Job pool only while that job's controller IsActive and not idling (Wood/Stone/Ore split, Forage, Smelt, Hunt, Farm, Fish, Cook, Upkeep new). Idling -> Idle; following (GetFollow and a follow target) -> Follow, 25% Idle; Stay/Follow mode otherwise -> Idle.
- SpeechConfig v2 (Version field): pools Action, Wood, Stone, Ore, Gather (fallback), Forage, Combat, Follow, Hungry, Repair, Overweight, Smelt, Idle, Hunt, Farm, Fish, Cook, Upkeep, NoBed, Chat. Lines can start with "[tag,tag]" (home/away via MHR_ToolKeeper.AtHomeRange and a set home, night/day EnvMan.IsNight, rain/dry EnvMan.IsWet, cold EnvMan.IsCold, indoors = InInterior or a roof within 30 m up, biomes via WorldGenerator.GetBiome; "!" = not). Lines that assumed an event ("Another one falls!", "A fine catch!", "Got one in my sights") or a place ("A fine hall you've built" without [home]) were dropped or tagged. speech.json with Version < 2 -> copied to speech.v1.json and replaced; a v2 file gets missing pools from the defaults (written back); Translations/speech/<lang>.json is kept as is (missing pools from English).
- Problems routed through SayProblem so far: ToolKeeper need/broke tool, Fish (need rod, bait, no water, pack full), Farm no cultivator, gathering overweight, radial repair without a hammer. Idle chat (IdleController) now from SpeechConfig.Chat with tags (was hc_idle_chat_1..6, "Smells like rain" at any time).
- No-bed lines (user: "the NPC would always say 'all the beds are taken', when out wandering out at night. Constantly."): CompanionRest sets _needsBed when the claimed Bed object is null - far from home the bed's zone isn't loaded, so it read as "no bed". NeedsBed() now also needs ZDO HasHomeBedHash false, and the NoBed small talk only plays at night AND at home (every 10 min at most).
- User test (log ac08f35b): "It seems to have worked." No mod errors; [Config] removed the 2 old Speech lines; speech.json upgraded (speech.v1.json kept); lines heard: Order "As you command."/"So it shall be."/"Heading over.", Event "I need to empty my bags first."/"Crops are ready!", Ambient "Which way now?" (following), "Farm work is honest work." (farming) - about one ambient line per several minutes. "The NPC did seem to stutter a bit when inside. It was like the NPC did not know what to do to idle." Log: Follow (Hunt+Follow) indoors at home while the player went between chests - "[Move|Follow] taking the 34m/27m/30m/28m way round, not straight (8-11m): something in the way", routes of 24-30 corners out through the door; plus a stuck approach to the throne (Settle -> chair, "standing still 3s - stepping back"). Cause: MHR_Follow aims at lead = spot + Travel x max(1.5, min(speed, 8)) while the player moves; indoors that point is outside the walls. Fix: indoors lead = 1 m; any lead without line of sight from the spot falls back to the spot.
- User test (log 5369679b): "I think it looks fine." No errors; Follow sat on the throne and "got up (player moved)" when the player walked off. Asked: "If the NPC sits down ... it can stay there for a bit while I am running around indoors. Maybe a distance check of like 10m". MHR_Follow.Tick: indoors the seat is kept while the player is within [Follow] SitStayRange (10 m) of the companion (was: player moved 2.5 m from where they stood); outdoors unchanged (1 m); stepping outside flips Indoors so it gets up. Also "Crops are ready!" x3 in a row: Event lines with a topic now repeat at most every 120 s (Farm harvest/plant use topics).
- Next (phase 2): why/give-up lines for the silent cases (movement give-ups, hunting, chests, seeds, honey reserve, refused orders...), the idle "why" with the real reason, the hover status line, announcements for a companion that dies or is lost far away.

### 1.5.4 - spear hunting, sneaking stamina, tool trips (user, 2026-10-13)
- Bug report (not on GitHub): the NPC walked up to a deer and stabbed at it with the spear (little or no damage), then threw the spear once the deer was far away. User test log 30669d4c: first deer - spear was its only weapon (no throw by design: a throw needs another weapon in the pack), "[Hunt] Sneaking up", "[Direct] attack" from the player, "[Move|Hunt] jumping the shallow water" while sneaking, "Stopped sneaking - spotted by deer", a running chase, then three stabs at 13/104 stamina. Later deer (sword back in the pack): "Took out its Flint Spear", "Threw ... (12 m)", picked it back up - the throw itself works.
- Code: the spear counted as melee for the approach (dist 0), so the hunter aimed for the deer itself and threw only if a throw happened on the way through 3-12 m; anything delaying it (facing, sight, stamina) ended in a stab at < 3 m. And the game refuses an attack it can't pay for (Attack.Start -> HaveStamina), with nothing logged.
- Fixes: MHR_SpearThrow.ReadyToThrow (spear in hand, skittish prey, another weapon, no spear out) -> UpdateCombat's approach distance = Standoff() ([Hunting] ThrowStandoff 8 m, clamped to 3.5..ThrowRange-1). WantsThrow also needs the stamina for the secondary attack (m_attackStamina; HaveStamina is patched to CompanionStamina) - else "[Hunt] Not throwing yet: not enough stamina ..." (Debug, every 5 s) and it waits at the standoff.
- Sneaking stamina (user: "the NPC should be conserving stamina when sneaking and try to avoid jumping. They can also pause to recover stamina."): CompanionCombatAI.HuntMove - while _stalking, below SneakRestStamina (0.3) it stops crouched facing the prey until SneakResumeStamina (0.6) (CompanionStamina drains sneak stamina only when moving). Chase (not stalking): run only above ChaseRunStamina (0.3), else SpeedJog.
- MHR_Mover: S.Quiet = CompanionStamina.IsCrouching, set every Go. Quiet: no water jumps (wades; a swim-depth crossing still swims), no gap leaps, step hops only after 1.5 s held up (was 0.5 s or any time going straight).
- Direct attack on game (user: "It seemed like the NPC did not want to sneak up on deer even when I tried to command it"): CompanionAI.DirectEngage called Engage, not EngagePrey, so HuntMode was off and UpdateTarget dropped a passive animal with no target of its own ("isn't a threat - leaving it"). Now a passive target (MHR_Threat.IsPassive) goes through EngagePrey (sneak, throw, loot). ThrowStandoff default 10 m (user: "Can the NPC throw from farther than 8m away?"); it throws anywhere from 3 m to ThrowRange (12).
- User test (log 32bd3a15): "The sneaking up on animals while hunting seems much better"; "the boar at the end of the session seemed to be missed with the first attack". Log: deer 1 (Direct attack -> hunt now) rested at 30%, sneaked on at 60%, threw from 13 m, picked the spear up. Deer 2 after the player took the sword (spear only -> stab by design): one sneak stab, Spears raised, down. Boar (MonsterAI, not skittish -> melee), 1 m above him on a slope: stab 1 has no "raised Spears" (skill gains only on a hit) and "spotted" comes after it - a miss; stabs 2 and 3 hit.
- Melee aim (checked in Attack.GetMeleeAttackDir/DoMeleeAttack): the sweep starts at the hand (attack origin + m_attackHeight), runs along transform.forward rotated towards GetAimDir (= look dir for a non-player) by at most m_maxYAngle, spread over m_attackAngle; hits with Dot(point - origin, attackDir) <= 0 are skipped. Ours looked at GetTopPoint (vanilla MonsterAI style) and swung once within m_aiAttackMaxAngle of the feet. Now: look at GetCenterPoint; closer than [Advanced] MeleeTooClose (0.3 m from the target's edge, num3) -> step back (MoveTowards away) instead of swinging, Debug "[Combat] Too close to swing at ..." every 5 s; an attack with m_attackAngle < 40 (stab) needs facing within max(8, angle/2 + 5) (capped at m_aiAttackMaxAngle).
- Attack log (user: "Should we add some logging around it to better see what is happening? At least until we have fixed everything."): Debug "[Attack] Swing|Shot at X: weapon, gap to its edge (num3), height difference, facing angle off vs limit, reach, sweep width (m_attackAngle), tilt (m_maxYAngle), sneaking / hasn't noticed" on each successful DoAttack in the creature branch; "Throw at X: distance, height, prey speed" from MHR_SpearThrow.Throw. Patches/CompanionHitLog: Character.Damage prefix (runs on the attacker's side for melee sweeps and projectiles), attacker = companion -> CompanionCombatAI.NoteHit -> "[Attack] Swing hit X for N after T s" (total damage before resistances). No hit within 1.2 s (swing) / 4 s (shot, throw), checked at the top of UpdateCombat or at the next swing -> "[Attack] ... missed X (now d m away, h m up, hp)". Temporary - part of the BACKLOG log cleanup once fights are sorted.
- User test (log 9fa8a7bb): "It looks better I think". [Attack] lines: flint spear = sweep 40 deg, tilt 0 deg (m_maxYAngle 0: the stab can't angle up or down at all - it goes level at hand height, so a target well above or below is missed; the earlier boar was +1.0 m up), aiAttackMaxAngle 5, reach 2.0 m. Hits: greydwarf 6.0, neck 8.1 (one stab kill), boar 11.2 (one stab kill), all at 1.7-2.0 m and 0-2 deg off. One "Too close (0.2 m) - stepping back" on a greydwarf. Second greydwarf stab had no result until the next fight ("Swing missed ?" - the target was gone and CheckMiss only ran in UpdateCombat): now checked every frame (CompanionCombatAI.Update), with the name kept and "it's gone" / "something else killed it".
- User test (log 5e188748): "the NPC did not see the deer that was very close to us. Ended up picking one far away and ran after it." Log: deer 1 (32 m) - sneaked from 32 m, rested 3 times (60% -> 30% in about 5-8 m of sneaking each time), then "Lost Deer - leaving it for a while" (HuntController: combat no longer engaged with the prey -> Skip 60 s); deer 2 at 27 m picked next, rest at 18% straight away, spotted, got away. Cause of the drop: UpdateTarget clears the target silently after _timeSinceAttacking > 60 s - a long stalk never attacks. FindPrey picks the nearest within range, so the close deer was most likely the skipped one.
- Fixes: hunting gets 180 s without an attack (fights still 60 s), and a Debug line says when/why the target is given up ("lost sight and sound of it for 30 s" / "no attack in N s"). [Hunting] SneakFrom (20 m): UpdateStalk only crouches within it (+2 m hysteresis), "Too far off to sneak yet - walking towards X"; beyond it HuntMove walks (no run) towards unaware prey. CompanionStamina: sneak drain x Lerp(1, 0.25, sqrt(Sneak skill factor)) like Player.OnSneaking (no equipment modifier).
- Prey swap (user: "yes, switch to a closer deer if it hasn't attacked yet"): CompanionCombatAI._attackedTarget (false on Engage, true in NoteSwing - swings, shots, throws). HuntController.UpdateHunting every 2 s while not attacked: FindPrey (nearest that needs meat, not skipped, no swim) - if it is another animal under 60% of the current distance and 5 m+ closer -> EngagePrey(closer), "[Hunt] Going for a closer X (d m) instead of the Y D m away".
- User test (log 30ffe411): "I think it looks fairly good. It looked like he got close to a deer then ran at it? I assume it had noticed him." Log: deer 2 - sneaking, two rests, "Stopped sneaking - spotted", then the chase (spear only - no sword in the pack, so no throw), one stab at 1.9 m missed as it bolted (now 3.9 m away, 10 hp), got away. Greydwarfs: 7 hits, 1 miss at 1.4 m (it moved). No errors. Sneaking still needs two breathers over 20 m at low Sneak skill (drain 5/s at skill ~0, like an unskilled player).
- Spear-only throw (user: "Throw the spear when hunting timid animals when noticed."): MHR_SpearThrow.MayThrow = another weapon in the pack OR the prey noticed us (BaseAI alerted or has a target) - used by ReadyToThrow (standoff), WantsThrow and EquipForHunt. After a throw with no weapon left (UnarmedAfterThrow: spear out, HasOtherWeapon(hum, null) false): CompanionCombatAI disengages the hunt ("Threw its only weapon at X - going to pick the spear up first"; the hunt job logs "Lost X" and skips it 60 s) and HuntController starts no new hunt until Drive has it back (or gives up after 120 s / no landing in 15 s).
- User test (log 80cd4b1f): "missed a close stab on a deer again"; "The range for hunting while on follow might be a little low. It feels like I see a deer ... and the NPC does not until we are much closer." Log: boar - spotted, spear-only throw from 11 m hit 16.8, fetched the spear, boar died (loot). Deer - two breathers, stab at an UNAWARE deer from 2.0 m off its edge (reach 2.0 = min(aiAttackRange, m_attackRange + 0.5)) missed; then a throw from 5 m at a deer running 6.2 m/s hit 17.5, spear fetched. Fixes: [Hunting] FollowRange default 40 (was 25; existing configs keep their value); against unaware targets the melee reach is capped to [Advanced] SneakStabReach - meters from the target's edge, 1.5 (user: "lets set the reach at 1.5. That should be plenty close enough to use the spear").
- Sneak-stab limit removed again (user: "I was thinking the sneak limit would be 1.8. An overall limit is fine. I dont think the sneak needs another limit over the top. Its something we will need to review for combat"): no [Advanced] SneakStabReach; only MeleeReachMargin for every swing.
- Reach (user: "we would be fine using 1.8 and forcing the NPC to ensure it is under 2.0 rather than being able to attack right at 2.0"): melee reach = MHR_MeleeReach (min(m_aiAttackRange, m_attackRange + 0.5), at least 1) minus [Advanced] MeleeReachMargin 0.2 (at least 0.8). The [Attack] swing line now also logs the attack type, m_attackRange, m_aiAttackRange, m_attackRayWidth (+ m_attackRayWidthCharExtra) and m_attackHeight, to find the real numbers per weapon for the combat review.
- User test (log 11cccd30): "I think it looks good. Lets build the zips." Flint spear numbers from the new log: Horizontal sweep 40 deg, tilt 0 deg, m_attackRange 1.90, m_aiAttackRange 2.00 (so reach = 2.0 - margin 0.2 = 1.8), hit width 0.50 (+0 vs creatures), hit height 1.50 m. Six spear-only throws after being spotted, 12-13 m (one 4 m), prey up to 6.1 m/s - all six hit (13-17 dmg), spear fetched each time. One sneak stab at a Neck 1.7 m off, 0.8 m BELOW him, missed - the level stab at 1.5 m height can't tilt down (combat review: per-weapon height window).
- Tool trips (user: "they kept trying to go back to home to get a bow it looked like. Even after I turned off hunt."): log - Hunt + Follow near home; the player took the crossbow out of the pack (inventory panel) and ToolKeeper went 58 m for BowHuntsman, then for BowFineWood; Hunt turned off (7 -> 0) and the fetch trip carried on, resumed after each recall ("Walking to chest: 51.8m left" ...). Fixes in MHR_ToolKeeper.TickOne: on a job change a fetch (Phase 1) for a kind the new job doesn't need ends ("Not fetching ... any more"); kinds the job uses that were in the pack when the inventory panel opened and are gone when it closes go into State.Refused ("You took its bow - it won't fetch another for this job"), skipped by the fetch loop until the job changes (a running fetch for that kind ends too). Ammo, bait and food aren't tracked (they get used up).

### 1.5.3 - world ladders (user log, 2026-10-12: "I finally found a spot with ladders and a mob at the top. The NPC was spinning around the bottom having a hard time finding a way up.")
- Log: Black Forest ruin (~(-2284, 35.3, -378)), skeleton up at 41.4-45.4; [Move|Combat] "4.7m below where it needs to be and no ladder found (tall pieces close by: )" - the list only named Pieces and was empty: the ladders are part of the location. The candidate loops took a non-Piece collider's root = the whole location (IsLadder then saw any AutoJumpLedge in the location, bounds = the whole ruin). Then partial routes and a stuck sidestep for ~30 s at the foot.
- Fix: LadderRoot(col) - Piece if any, else the nearest of the collider's object and up to 3 parents named "ladder" or holding an AutoJumpLedge, only if ladder-sized (< 14 m tall, not > 4 m both ways); used by the climb and descend searches, stack joining and the headroom check. The "no ladder found" diagnostic now lists world objects as parent/object names (it says "tall things"), so if these ladders are something else (a use-to-climb Ladder, another component) the next log names them. Not confirmed in game - the ruin's ladder prefab isn't in the decompiled code.
- User test 1.5.3 ("The NPC went up one flight then jumped off."): same Black Forest ruin as the last log - NOT player built (user correction): the location is made of the game's own building pieces (wood_stepladder, wood_floor, stone_wall_*), so they show up as Pieces and the ladders were found this time; [Move|Combat] after a skeleton. Climbed (-2280.6, 36.3) 35.3 -> 37.4, then "jumping from the top of the ladder onto (-2281.3, 37.1, -378.9) (-0.3m up, 0.8m on)" and was back at 35.3 - MHR_Jump.Leap's full running jump for a 0.8 m level step carried him over the small floor. Fix (LadderTopHop): ClimbDir (where the ladder's top floor is) before the goal direction; a spot needs footing 0.5 m further on within 0.4 m of its height; level (< 0.4 m up) and <= 1.3 m away -> walk 0.5 s onto it ("stepping off the top of the ladder onto"), no jump; the nothing-found fallback walks off for 0.5 s instead of a 3 m/s leap (LedgeAhead still halts over a harmful drop).
- User test ("Sort of better. There was a stone block that was overhanging the 2nd ladder ... They keep going off to one side rather than straight up to the next floor."): the ruin's ladders slope like stairs (2 m up over ~3 m) and zigzag in a shaft - L1 foot (-2279.8, 35.3, -376.5) -> top end around (-2281.2, 37.4, -379.7); L2 centre (-2279.6, 38.3, -378.9) climbs towards +z; L3 foot (-2279.8, 39.4, -376.5). L2: started (-2280.4, 37.4, -380.2), "climbed" at (-2278.1, 39.3, -374.7) - 2.7 m past its top end, because the 0.8 s "still being lifted?" wait kept steering along ClimbDir. Then "the foot of the ladder at (-2279.8, 39.4, -376.5) is past a drop" from 0.64 m away - the 0.6 m look-ahead passed the foot into the shaft hole. Fix: during that wait, Halt while on the ground (keep steering only while airborne in the bounce); the walk-to-foot drop check looks min(0.6, distance to the foot) ahead. Overhanging stone block on L2: part of the ruin (a player can't jump up there either), left.
- User test ("Again sort of better."): L1 then L2 fine, L2 now ended at (-2278.8, 39.4, -376.7) - but LadderTopHop then stepped him on to (-2278.5, -375.9), and the walk to L3's foot (-2279.8, 39.4, -376.5) "is past a drop" (a hole between, the shaft); edge guard 4.1 m; TryHopUp onto 40.0 and off -> ground. Fix: at the top of a ladder with the goal still > 1.5 m up, TryStartLadderClimb first (the just-climbed ladder is in the tried set), LadderTopHop only if nothing; the walk-to-foot drop check now needs no ground within 4 m below (a harmful drop), not 1.1 m - the shaft hole is ~4 m and harmless.
- User test ("The 3rd ladder from the 2nd floor up the NPC steps to the edge of the ladder rather than going straight up it."): L2 top (-2279.0, 39.4, -377.1), L3 chosen at once (2.1 m), still "past a drop" - the floor 39.4 over ground 35.3 is a 4.1 m drop and the 4.5 m ray from +0.5 reached only 4.0 m down; one sample 0.6 m ahead landed in the opening L2 comes up through. Then hop-ups beside L3 and back to the ground. Fix: within 1.2 m of the foot no check; further, sample every 0.2 m up to 1.4 m with a 5.5 m ray and give up only on 3 misses in a row (a >= 0.6 m wide hole deeper than ~5 m). Walk to the foot while > 0.6 m off for the first 6 s (was 3 s), then while > 1.2 m (was 1.5) - pushing ClimbDir from 1+ m beside the foot walked him along the ladder's side.
- User test ("the ladders and stairs look like one continuous path to me. The NPC did finally get up them. I then walked up them at the very end and the NPC still had issues following me."): combat climb made it to L4 (41.4 floor), L3 once more "past a drop" from 2.1 m (fine from 1.4 m). Follow at the end: path-walk joined at the LAST on-level step, 2.4 m away across the shaft; the mover approach hit the edge guard (6.1 m) twice. Fix: join at the latest step within 1.5 m flat / 0.5 m height first (then walk the steps directly), else the old rule; the walk-to-foot gap check only beyond 2.5 m from the foot.
- User: "There is no shaft or gap up the ladder. I am not sure how the NPC is detecting what is there but its wrong. As long as the NPC stays directly on the ladder and floor sections there is no gap." Right - the checks look along the straight line from where he stands to where he's heading; on the narrow floors that line cuts across the open side, so the "drop" is real but only because he cuts the corner (my "shaft opening" wording was wrong). Fix (Climb walk-to-foot): FloorAlong(a, b) = floor or ladder within 1 m below every 0.2 m (2 misses in a row = off the floor); if the straight line fails, FindFloorVia: 16 directions x 0.6/1.05/1.5 m, a point on our level with FloorAlong to it and from it to the foot, shortest total - walk there first (ClimbVia, 3 s), Dbg "going round by ... keeping to the floor"; none -> "no floor all the way to the foot of the ladder ... - trying another". Replaces the deep-drop gap test and the 2.5 m no-check radius. The mover's edge guard (LedgeAhead) on normal routes is unchanged.
- User: "going up a ladder using a straight line will not work ... Its just like the open door issue we had before. Where the NPC needs to walk to the front of the ladder rather than b-line for it." Design rule for ladders (like DoorwayLine for doors): get to the foot spot in front of the ladder by the floor, line up with it, then walk straight in along its direction - never a straight line from wherever he stands. Current code: foot spot = in front of the bottom end (LadderFoot), walked to on the floor (FloorAlong/FindFloorVia), then pushed along ClimbDir. If it still cuts in: add a line-up point further back along -ClimbDir (as doorways do) before the foot.
- User test ("Well that was better. Still stops at the wall and sits there while going up."): Follow climb reached L5 top 45.4 (player at 45.6) then "jumping from the top of the ladder onto (-2281.5, 46.3, -380.7) (0.9m up, 0.8m on)" -> "fell 11.3 m and took 45 fall damage" (over the tower wall). Combat climb: L1-L4 to 43.1, L4 start "standing still 4s, no closer for 5s" at the wall by its foot; L5 "no floor all the way to the foot" from 1.2 m and "stopping at the edge (3.7m down)" on the ladder - line probes falling between the stair-like ladder's rungs. Fixes: FloorAlong, FindFloorVia and LedgeAhead's near-ground test use SphereCast r 0.25 (foot-sized); walk-to-foot tracks the best distance (ClimbFootBest/At) and stops walking there after 1.5 s without getting 0.15 m closer (then pushes in / helper jumps); LadderTopHop skips spots > 0.4 m up unless the goal is > 1 m above us, and a leap is refused when LedgeAhead from the landing (1 m on) finds a harmful drop - steps onto level floor are not.
- User test ("That was much better! The NPC just looks a little awkward getting up the ladder as it pauses on each landing before going up the next set."): combat climb L1-L5 in a row, "jumping onto the ladder (try 1/4)" at every ladder. Pause per landing = 0.8 s top wait + 1 s waiting for a lift before the helper jump. Now 0.5 s at the top, 0.4 s at the foot (pos < start + 0.3), 1 s part way up.
- User test ("can they start to turn in the direction they need to move next while they wait ... The NPC fell off the ladder and then was having a hard time finding the same path back up."): at L2's top (39.4) the skeleton was at 41.4 - TryStartLadderClimb returned at needY < pos.y + 2 (2.0 exactly), so LadderTopHop stepped him off and he walked off (fell 4.0 m, 0 damage). Back on L1's top: "no ladder found" - L2 was still in LadderClimbsTried (cleared only on Reset). Fixes: search from needY >= pos.y + 1.5; LadderClimbsTried cleared after every successful climb; during the top wait he turns (360 deg/s) towards NextFootDir - the foot of the nearest other ladder starting on this level within 3.5 m, else the goal - computed once per climb.
- User ("Still pauses at the top of the ladder. It just does not flow well. Is there a better way ...?" -> agreed design, "Yes, build it"). Log: L1 fine, then fell off L2, then looped at the bottom ("keeps ending up back where it started", 4 jump tries). The per-ladder cycle (find -> walk to foot -> push/jump -> top -> wait -> look around -> find next) paused on every landing and gave a wrong choice a chance at each.
- New: ladder chains (MHR_Mover). TryStartLadderClimb first tries UseKnownChain (a remembered chain with a foot within 3 m / 1 m height of us whose last top is > 1.5 m up -> start there); otherwise after choosing the first ladder PlanChain: from each ladder's top (centre + Dir x depth at max.y) the nearest ladder within 4 m whose foot (LadderFoot) is within 1 m of that top's height and 3.5 m of it and that goes > 1 m higher, until the top is within 0.8 m of needY (12 max). 2+ ladders -> StartChain at once (foot <= 3 m on our level) or after the LadderPending approach (PendingChain). RunChain each tick: top reached (pos.y >= Top - 0.6) -> next step immediately (Dbg "next ladder (i/n)"), last one -> RememberChain (15 min, 20 max), "up the ladders", 0.5 s step off along its Dir unless LedgeAhead. Per step: walk to the foot by the floor (FloorAlong/FindFloorVia), "reached" once within 0.6 m or no closer for 1 s, then steer along Dir and hop on at once (jumps every 0.7 s; part way up only after 0.6 s without rising); > 6 jumps without rising or 6 s without progress -> bad 120 s, EndChain. Fell (> 1 m below the current foot) -> back on at the step whose foot is on this level within 6 m, else EndChain. Reset keeps the chain while the goal is > 1 m up; OnTheWayUp includes it; Follow's path walk doesn't take over a chain (OnLadderChain). Single ladders keep the old code.
- User test of chains ("Ok better. Still a bit wonky and the NPC had issues getting down the ladder."): up: "climbing a chain of 2 ladders up to 39.4", "fell off the ladders - back on at ladder 1/2", "up the ladders", then "climbing a chain of 3 ladders up to 45.4" - the first plan stopped at 2 because L3's foot was just past 3.5 m from L2's estimated top. Down: still the single-ladder Descend - "went down the ladder to (-2280.0, 40.2, ...)" (ends at foot + 1 m on these sloping ladders, bottom 39.4), then edges and "dropping down ... (4.1m)".
- Fixes: PlanChain searches 5 m round the top and takes feet up to 4.5 m from it. Down chains: TryLadderDown plans PlanChainDown from the chosen ladder (each next ladder's top within 1.2 m of this one's bottom and its step-on spot within 4.5 m of this one's foot, going > 1 m lower, until within 0.8 m of the goal's height); 2+ -> StartChain(down) at once (top spot <= 3 m, our level) or after the pending approach (PendingChain + PendingDown). LadderStep.TopSpot = centre + Dir x (depth + 0.5) at max.y, snapped to the floor. RunChainDown: on the landing walk to TopSpot by the floor ("there" within 0.45 m or no closer for 1 s), then walk towards the Foot (down the ladder) at walking pace, no jumps; done at Bottom + 0.6 on the ground -> next ladder at once ("next ladder down (i/n)"), last -> "down the ladders"; fell more than 1 m past a ladder -> carry on from the ladder whose top is on this level; 6 s without getting 0.3 m lower -> bad 120 s ("stuck going down ladder"). Reset keeps a down chain while the goal is > 1 m below.
- User test (no log first, then 0ea26966): "following me even though follow was off. They jumped off the top of the tower again ... trying to jump up the ladder rather than just go up them and let the game bump them ... struggled to get onto the ladder ... pause as it gets to the top ... trying to run into the wall ... did not finish going up the ladders to get to the same floor as the mob."
  - Follow off: [Setup] ApplyFollowMode follow=False -> "Follow OFF", then [Return] OnSpawned -> Trigger: IsInFollowMode only checks ActionMode == 0, so it SetFollow(true) + SetFollowTarget(player); back in range it SetFollow(prev=false) - the ZDO flag only, the AI follow target stayed the player. Fix: Trigger skips companions whose Follow toggle is off; on finishing it also calls ApplyFollowMode(mode, true).
  - "fell 12.2 m and took 51 fall damage": path walk at (-2282.7, 46.9, ...) - on the wall top above the 45.4 floor - its jump-when-the-next-step-is-up put him there. Now: jump only after 1 s without progress, and not when the ground 0.6 m ahead is > 0.5 m above the next step (a wall top).
  - Combat chain of 5: "fell off the ladders - back on at ladder 1/5" x3, 3/5 x2, 2/5 x2 - the step switch at Top - 0.6 turned him for the next foot while still on the slope. Now: switch only on the ground at Top - 0.3, or at Top - 0.8 after 0.5 s without rising (a wall at the top).
  - Jumping: chain hop-on at the foot was immediate; now walk in first - hop after 1 s at the foot without a lift, 1.5 s part way up (ChainPushAt).
  - Command down chains worked ("going down a chain of 4 ladders ... down the ladders").
- User: "There is no reason to have the NPC sprint up the ladder. Save the stamina ... Walk into the ladder and push directly forward to go up the ladder. As this is a zigzag turn 90 degrees to move in front of the next ladder. Rinse and repeat" + "He stopped as the NPC was attack the skeleton. Since the NPC was not at the right level it was hitting just the ladder ... Please be sure to check the entire log." Full log (0ea26966): stamina 0-11% through the whole climb (HaveStamina/UseStamina FAILED), then at 43.4 with the skeleton at 45.4 ([Balanced] dist 2.8) six sword swings (7.8 stamina each, durability drain) into the ladder until the user commanded him down. Fixes: CompanionCombatAI melee in-range (flag8) also needs the target's feet within +1.4 / -1.8 m of ours (ranged unchanged) - otherwise it keeps chasing (the ladder chain); RunChain and single-ladder Climb steer at walking pace (pit climbs still run). [Balanced] status line (30/session) Info -> Debug. Rest of the log: Return/Follow fixed above; Repair scans, Interact/AIGate/Radial normal (user toggled Follow on and off, then a Direct move); command down chain fine.
- User log d49b9890 ("still having trouble"), read in full: load -> Follow off, [Return] "skipped - Follow is off" (fix works); skeleton fight from the 37.4 landing: "climbing a chain of 3 ladders up to 43.4" -> ladder 3 at 42.1 -> "fell off ... (now at (-2279.1, 38.2, -373.8))" outside the tower; again "climbing a chain of 5" -> ladder 4 from (-2281.0, 41.4, -380.0) -> (-2278.4, 33.4, -371.4) "fell 9.7 m and took 36 fall damage" (hp 30%). L2/L4 climb towards +z, toward an opening in the wall: once lifted the runner steered along Dir until "top" (on the ground at Top - 0.3), so he walked past the top end and out. Then ~60 s at the bottom "no ladder found" next to L1 - LadderClimbsTried still held it (only cleared after single climbs). No melee swings at the ladder this time (height check works).
- Fixes: LadderStep.Run = distance along Dir from the foot spot to the ladder's top end; pastTop = foot reached, within 1.2 m of Top, and either > Run + 0.3 along Dir or LedgeAhead(0.6) along Dir -> counts as the top (on the ground; in the air it halts). EndChain clears LadderClimbsTried.
- User log 47e712a7 ("I thought there was an edge check? ... open air stair with no walls ... We are going backwards. The NPC keeps trying to jump up the ladder. Pauses and runs into the wall at the top. Then is drifting to the side and jumping off ... Did the door fix not get implemented in this new planning? They do not seem to be following the process we talked about."). Read in full: Follow off respected; chain of 5 from the ground; at L4's foot (-2281.0, 41.4, -380.0) -> walked along +z at floor level to (-2280.4, 41.4, -377.2) and off the open side: "fell 9.3 m and took 33 fall damage" (hp 8%), then [Flee] "triggered"/"could not create distance" every tick (~70 lines). Causes: (1) RunChain bypassed the mover's edge guard (LedgeAhead lives in GoInner's normal steering only); (2) no line-up - straight at the foot, at an angle; (3) the ladder never lifted him on any ladder in any log - the AutoJumpLedge trigger apparently only reacts to players (monsters never use these ladders), so every climb fell back to hops.
- Fixes: LadderBump(c, ledges, pos): inside an AutoJumpLedge trigger (ClosestPoint within 0.5 m of pos + 0.5 up) -> c.OnAutoJump(ledge.forward, m_upVel, m_forwardVel), the call the game makes for a player (it checks ground, the 60 deg direction and its own 0.5 s spacing). Chains: LadderStep.Ledges; at the foot / on the ladder the runner walks in and bumps every tick, no jumps at all; 4 s at the foot without a lift -> "couldn't get onto ladder" (bad 120 s). Line-up like DoorwayLine: first to Foot - Dir x 0.8 (needs floor within 0.6 m of the foot's height; skipped when already coming in within ~37 deg of square), then the foot. Edge check: on a landing (not lifted, on the ground) LedgeAhead(0.5) along the steering direction -> Halt (Dbg "edge ahead on the ladders"). Single-ladder Climb: S.ClimbLedges, bump while pushing in; hop only for a bottom up off the floor or after 2 s. [Flee] lines once per 5 s.
- User log 3ef917f2 ("It looks like the hop is not getting triggered. Its ok to have the NPC jump if they are stuck."): read in full - Gunter had died in the last test, [Resurrect] from the tombstone at the bottom (23 items back, Follow restored on); skeleton fight: "climbing a chain of 5 ladders", then 4 s at L1's foot with no lift -> "couldn't get onto ladder 1/5", then unstuck steps at the bottom for the rest. The OnAutoJump bump didn't lift him; why is unknown (not in the trigger by our 0.5 m test, or OnAutoJump refused - ground, direction, spacing). Now: LadderBump(.., out why) checks every trigger collider under the ledge (children too, 0.6 m) and reports the nearest distance, or onGround / dot(m_moveDir via AccessTools, ledge forward) / m_upVel / m_forwardVel when it calls OnAutoJump - Dbg once per ladder "ladder i/n bump: ..." after 1 s at it. Hop fallback back in the chain runner: 1.5 s without rising at the foot or on the ladder -> Jump + 2.5 m/s along the steering direction, every 1 s, not under 20% stamina, 6 max -> "couldn't get up ladder" (bad 120 s).
- User log 2d87d7fa ("Went up and down the same ladder a few times. Then got about 1/2 way up the tower and jumped completely off to the ground."): read in full. Bump diagnostics: "ladder 1/5 bump: nearest lift zone 1.24m away", "ladder 3/5 ... 1.17m", "ladder 4/5 ... 2.03m", and on ladder 2 "in the lift zone, bumped (onGround=True, walking-direction match 0.00, up 6.0 fwd 3.0)" - OnAutoJump refused (Dot(m_moveDir, dir) < 0.5, m_moveDir zero at that point). "fell off the ladders - back on at ladder 1/5" x4 right after "next ladder (2/5)": the line-up spot behind L2's foot was on L1 (zigzag) - walking to it went back down L1. Ladder 4: 2.03 m from the foot, "blocked" (no closer 1 s) counted as at the foot -> pushed/hopped along +z, the hop carried him over the open side: "fell 9.5 m and took 34 fall damage".
- Fixes: LadderBump sets c.SetMoveDir(ledge forward) before OnAutoJump. LiftZoneEdge: the foot spot (chain steps and single ladders) moves to just short (0.2 m) of the nearest point of the ledge's trigger, on the floor (within 0.6 m height), and Run is adjusted. The line-up spot is skipped when the floor under it is a ladder. "Blocked/reached/atFoot" by no-closer-for-1 s only within 1.2 m of the foot. Hops are refused when LedgeAhead at 0.8 or 1.6 m along the hop direction.
- User test 3fa2b3c6 ("That looked much better than before. The bump seems to kind of work now. The NPC also did a much better job of just pathing up and getting in front of the ladder."): read in full - Follow off respected; skeleton fight: "climbing a chain of 5 ladders up to 45.4" in one go, no falls; "ladder 1/5 bump: in the lift zone, bumped (onGround=True, walking-direction match 1.00, up 6.0 fwd 3.0), lifted"; ladders 2-5: bumped with match 1.00 but "not lifted yet" after 1 s -> one hop each, then up. Killed the skeleton (Blocking/Swords skill gains), looted Bone Fragments, then Command down: "going down a chain of 5 ladders" fine. Open: why the bump lifts on L1 but not L2-L5 (maybe the landing ladders' bottom rung is higher, or the bump fires before he's in the zone properly) - one hop per ladder for now.
- User: "Look into the hop on ladders 2 to 5. It works just fine when I use it. I walk into the ladder, continue to walk into it and it moves me up. When I stop walking into it I stop." Game code: OnAutoJump(dir, up, fwd) requires owner, on ground, not dead/attacking/dodging/knocked back and 0.5 s since m_lastAutoJumpTime - and SETS m_lastAutoJumpTime before checking Dot(m_moveDir, dir) >= 0.5. AutoJumpLedge.OnTriggerStay calls it every physics step for any Character inside (companions included); at that point a companion's m_moveDir reads zero (the 0.00 match seen earlier), so the game's call is refused but blocks the next 0.5 s - and the mover's LadderBump (direction set first) mostly fell inside the blocked window. L1 lifted by luck of timing, L2-5 "not lifted yet" -> one hop each. Fix: Patches/CompanionLadderBumpPatch - Harmony prefix on AutoJumpLedge.OnTriggerStay skips companions (players and other creatures unchanged); the mover's bump is the only one, every 0.5 s while it walks into the ladder, like a player. LadderBump's diagnostic notes when he was mid-swing (InAttack - no bump then, as for a player).
- User log 8454ffa1 ("And that is worse. Significantly worse."): read in full - idle/sitting, a Direct move to the tower, skeleton fight: "climbing a chain of 5 ladders", L1 "bumped ... not lifted yet" -> hop -> "next ladder (2/5)" -> stood at (-2280.7, 36.7, -378.7) ("standing still 5s") -> "stuck on ladder 2/5", three times. With the game's trigger skipped for companions nothing bumped him up L1's last rungs: the runner had switched to L2 (at Top - 0.8 after 0.5 s without rising - shorter than the 0.5 s bump spacing allows) and only bumped L2's zone, so he stood 0.7 m below the 37.4 landing.
- Fix: CompanionLadderBumpPatch now keeps the game's bump for companions - prefix: walking input flat < 0.05 or more than 60 deg off the ledge's forward -> skip (no refused attempt to block the next one); otherwise SetMoveDir(normalised input) so the game's Dot >= 0.5 check passes, then let it run. RunChain bumps the previous ladder's zone too when not in the current one; the "stalled near the top" switch now needs 1.2 s without rising.
- User log dceeb044 ("Seemed to work on the first ladder. Then not on the rest. The NPC also did not got all the way to the top before attacking the mob. Gunter stopped 1/2 way up the last ladder."): read in full - idle, Direct move to the tower, skeleton fight. L1 "bumped (onGround=False ...), lifted"; every later ladder "bumped (onGround=True, walking-direction match 1.00, up 6.0 fwd 3.0), not lifted yet" -> "stuck ... hopping (1)" at 1.5 s -> next ladder. The bump is accepted but each one lifts about a rung (~0.25 m) on these stair-like ladders; progress needed +0.3 m and "lifted" +0.4 m, so the rung-by-rung climb read as stuck. Last ladder: skeleton at the top edge, Gunter ~1 m below its feet -> inside the +1.4 m melee reach -> stopped and swung (5 swings, blocked hits) from the ladder until commanded down; Command down chains fine both times.
- Fixes: chain progress at +0.1 m, "lifted" at +0.25 m above the foot, the stuck hop only after 2.5 s without any rise. CompanionCombatAI: melee not "in range" while MHR_Mover.OnLadderChain and the target is > 0.5 m above - finish the climb onto its floor first.
- User log b574d5a1 ("It was a little bit better. The NPC keeps running off when there is nothing to do."): read in full. Ladders: chain of 5, hops only on L1 and L3 (twice), L2/L4 bumped up, L5 "lifted"; killed the skeleton, looted, Command down the chain of 5 fine. Running off: after the Direct move to (-2280.4, 35.3, -376.2) -> "[Idle|Gunter] Nothing to do - idling around (-2249.9, 34.8, -375.9)" - 30 m away; the start-of-log idle centre was also ~10 m off. IdleController.Begin used the home point whenever MHR_ToolKeeper.NearHome (HomeTripRange 60 m). Now: idle centre = where it stands, home only within 15 m of it.
- User log 646dd4f1 ("The running off seemed better. Its still looks like the NPC is getting stuck on the ladders. It works after a moment or 2. Then they get stuck on the next one."): the trace (read in full) - L1 from the ground: bumps accepted, climbing y 35.9 -> 37.4. Every landing ladder (2-5): "at the foot ... off=0.13 ... bumps: mine 1/6" -> "ground=False vy=6.0" -> 0.3 s later the same y and off, ground=True vy=0.0, over and over; never forward, never up; then "stuck ... hopping (1)" and the hop (jump + forward) works. The bump is accepted but he is stopped dead going up: the floor above overhangs the landing ladders' feet (the user's "stone block overhanging the 2nd ladder" is one) and the foot spot from LiftZoneEdge (0.2 m short of the trigger) sits under it. Fix: LiftZoneEdge steps from 0.2 m short of the trigger forward along the ladder (0.2 m steps, up to 1.2 m in) to the first spot with floor/rung under it and HeadRoom(2.2 m, ladder pieces ignored); trace prints headroom=yes/NO.
- User test 253a9da0 ("That is more like it. Ran it twice and both times it was nice and smooth."): read in full - two skeleton fights, each "climbing a chain of 5 ladders" straight through (next ladder 2..5/5, no hops, no stuck, no falls), the ladder trigger's bumps accepted on every ladder (refusals only "within 0.5 s", as for a player); Command down chains of 4 and 5 fine; after each move order "[Idle|Gunter] Nothing to do - idling around (-2281.0, 35.3, -376.9)" - right where he was sent (idle/spawn-wander fixes work). Ladders settled for 1.5.3. Cleanup (user: "Lets take it out"): removed Patches/CompanionLadderBumpTrace.cs, the RunChain [Ladder] trace line, LadderBump (the old own-bump, latterly report-only) with its per-ladder "bump:" line and the m_moveDir reflection, LadderStep.Ledges/BumpLogged and S.NextChainTrace. Kept: CompanionLadderBumpPatch, LiftZoneEdge/HeadRoom foot spot, chains up/down, edge checks, stuck hop.
- User: "Why does it work perfectly fine for the player but the NPC is getting stuck on the ladder. I thought the bump carried you up the ladder?" Game code: Character.UpdateWalking, while IsOnGround(), caps velocity.y at 3 (currentVel.y = Mathf.Min(currentVel.y, 3f)) before applying the velocity change. The ladder trigger bumps from the physics step (OnTriggerStay) and the character is airborne before the next UpdateWalking; the mover's own OnAutoJump call came from the AI while he was still touching the floor, so its 6 m/s became 3 - a bounce that doesn't clear the ladder's edge - and it took OnAutoJump's 0.5 s slot, so the trigger's full bump was refused "within 0.5 s" every time (trace: "game 0/15, mine 1/6" at every landing ladder). The mover no longer bumps: LadderBump only reports (zone distance, ground, direction match); the ladder's trigger bumps him as it does a player, with CompanionLadderBumpPatch making his walking input read full-strength along his heading when he walks the ladder's way (skipping the call otherwise). Trace now counts the trigger's bumps only ("bumps: ok/total accepted (refused: why)"). The quick hop at the foot (0.8 s) stays as the stuck fallback.
- User on the head-room theory: "that does not sound right. It looked like there was plenty of headroom. That the npc stood there bashing into the ladder then bouncing a bit then going up the ladder. Like the logic took a few seconds to figure out what it was supposed to do." Re-read: off stays 0.13 while he walks forward (pressed against the ladder), each accepted bump rises at 6 m/s and drops back to the same spot - the bump (~0.9 m) doesn't clear the ladder's bottom edge from there, a jump (~1.6 m + forward push) does. The "few seconds" was the 2.5 s stuck wait. Now at the foot the hop comes after 0.8 s without rising (part way up still 2.5 s). The head-room search in LiftZoneEdge stays (it keeps the old spot when there is head room).
- User: "for the idle the NPC should idle where it is, unless it is within 15m of its home range? The NPC was trying to run home even though we are very very very far away?" Yes to the rule. The 30 m trip wasn't home (beyond the old 60 m rule): no MHR_Mover lines between the Direct move ending and "[Idle|Gunter] Nothing to do - idling around (-2249.9, ...)", i.e. the idle centre was where he already stood. CompanionAI's no-follow/no-job branch called the game's BaseAI.IdleMovement -> RandomMovement around m_spawnPoint (not tamed), heading back once > 2 x randomMoveRange (20 m) away - his spawn point is where he was resurrected/created. That ran in the 10 s before IdleController starts (and whenever Idle is off). Replaced with StopMoving() there and in FollowWithFormation's null-target fallback.
- User: "We need to look at the ladders more closely. It was still wonky." Added a debug trace instead of another guess: Patches/CompanionLadderBumpTrace (prefix/postfix on Character.OnAutoJump for companions) counts calls from the game's ledge trigger vs the mover (CompanionLadderBumpTrace.FromMover around LadderBump's call) and, for refused ones, the game's reason in its own order (not on the ground, mid-swing, dodging, knocked back, within 0.5 s, walking-direction mismatch). RunChain prints every 0.3 s while on a chain: "[Ladder i/n] <lining up | to the foot | at the foot | climbing> y=.. (foot .., top ..) off=.. ground=.. vy=.. facing=.. bumps: game ok/total, mine ok/total (refused: ...)". Debug only; take it out once ladders are settled.
- Design rule (ladders, agreed): walk (not run) to a line-up spot square in front of the ladder -> its foot -> walk straight up letting the ladder bump him -> at the top stop on the landing (never past the top end or off an edge) -> turn to the next ladder -> repeat. Stamina watched (rest on landings). No jumping unless stuck (user: "Its ok to have the NPC jump if they are stuck") or a ladder that starts off the floor.
- Ladder chains and stamina: on a landing (not lifted, on the ground) below 15% stamina he stands until 40% (8 s at most) - Dbg "getting my breath back on the landing"; helper hops skipped below 25%. Never stops part way up a ladder.
- [Balanced] weapon-choice entry log (WeaponLogic.EntryLog, 200 per session) moved from Info to Debug - 136 lines in this log.

### 1.5.2 - melee hunting (user, 2026-10-12: a player's video of a spear hunter; "If they have no ranged weapon they should try to sneak up and attack whatever they are hunting.")
- CompanionCombatAI.UpdateCombat used the 30 m reach and 20 m standoff whenever HuntMode was on, whatever the weapon - a hunter with no bow ready stood 20 m off and stabbed at the air. Now only a ranged weapon gets them: flag6 (aiAttackRange > 10 or ItemType.Bow) or the new MHR_IsProjectileWeapon (Bow, any m_ammoType - crossbows - or a Projectile primary attack). Melee hunting closes to the weapon's own range; sneaking (UpdateStalk) is unchanged, so it creeps up while the prey is unaware.
- Sneaking (answered): only prey that runs away - AnimalAI (deer, hares...) or a MonsterAI with m_fleeIfNotAlerted - while it's not alerted and has no target. Anything else is approached normally.
- Spear throw (user: "make the NPC throw the spear when hunting one of the timid / flee animals ... only if they have another weapon in their inventory. The NPC should then go over and recover the spear."): Jobs/MHR_SpearThrow.cs. In CompanionCombatAI's attack step, while HuntMode: WantsThrow = [Hunting] ThrowSpear, current weapon's secondary attack is a consuming Projectile (spear throw), prey is skittish (AnimalAI or MonsterAI.m_fleeIfNotAlerted, alerted or not), 3 m <= distance <= ThrowRange (12), another usable weapon in the pack (1H/2H/2H-left/bow, not broken, no pickaxe damage, not a fishing rod), no spear already out, 2 s between tries. Stops, faces the prey's centre (game aims an AI projectile straight at the target's centre, no drop compensation - hence the modest range), Humanoid.StartAttack(prey, secondaryAttack: true). The game removes the spear (Attack.ConsumeItem) and the projectile drops it as an item where it hits (m_respawnItemOnHit, the same ItemData) - an ItemDrop.DropItem postfix matches that item and records the drop. MHR_SpearThrow.Drive runs in CompanionAI.UpdateAI after combat (before ToolKeeper and loot): walks to the drop and picks it up (RequestOwn/Load/Pickup like MHR_Loot); gives up on a long swim, no route, someone else taking it, no landing within 15 s, or 120 s total. A throw that never went off (staggered) is forgotten once the spear is seen still in the pack.
- User test of the throw (log f9caeef4, v1.5.2): no throw, "trying to stab from distance". Log: (1) deer hunt: "[Ranged] FindReadyBow -> $item_arrow_wood" - WeaponLogic.BowLike counted anything with an m_ammoType, arrows included, so TryForceRangedWeapon "succeeded" by equipping arrows and the melee pick never ran; Gunter fought with the sword (no spear in hand -> no throw). (2) bear hunt: vanilla EquipBestWeapon put the flint spear in hand; flag6 (m_aiAttackRange > 10 -> ranged) made the spear "ranged" (its AI range is the throw distance) -> 30 m reach, primary stab from 16 m. TryForceMeleeWeapon and TorchOpener also skipped spears by that test. (3) "[Rest] Its bed ... is gone" while 2700 m from home - the 1.5.1 load-wait gave the bed up when away.
- Fixes: BowLike and MHR_IsProjectileWeapon exclude Ammo/AmmoNonEquipable (and consumables); ranged (flag6, HuntMove, TryForceMeleeWeapon, TorchOpener) = MHR_IsProjectileWeapon only (Bow, a weapon with an ammo type, or a Projectile primary attack); melee reach = min(m_aiAttackRange, m_attack.m_attackRange + 0.5) (MHR_MeleeReach). Hunt weapon pick: ready bow -> MHR_SpearThrow.EquipForHunt (skittish prey, ThrowSpear on, no spear out, a throwable spear with another weapon in the pack - best damage) -> game pick + best melee; the spear check runs before the game's own pick so the spear isn't swapped out every second. Bed: when the claimed bed isn't found and its spot is > 40 m away, keep it (area not loaded) - the 60 s give-up only counts near it.
- User test 2 (log a13be64b): throws work - "Threw its Flint Spear at Deer (13 m)", landed, picked back up; a crocodile (modded, skittish) too. Feedback: "the aim with the spear throw is not great against deer"; an odd jump over water and straight back during a hunt chase (left for the combat/movement review); still looking for a wood ladder to test. "Its Flint Spear isn't on the ground any more (someone picked it up)" was the companion's own auto-pickup 3 m from where it landed - Drive now checks the pack for the item name and says "picked it back up on the way".
- Aim: the game aims an AI projectile straight at the target's centre (Attack.GetProjectileSpawnPoint, RotateTowards from facing), no lead, no drop. Postfix CompanionProjectileAimPatch (companions only, target = BaseAI target): speed = m_projectileVel (+ ammo's), gravity = the projectile prefab's Projectile.m_gravity (ammo's prefab if any); lead = target velocity x flight time (3 iterations); aim point raised by 0.5 g t^2. Applied only when within 25 deg of the game's aim (a stale/other AI target is left alone). Covers spears, arrows, bolts. The random spread from low weapon skill (m_skillAccuracy) is vanilla and unchanged.
- Ladder climb (user log 06b694c3: "travel to another outpost in the black forest and the NPC was having a hard time going up the ladders to follow me"): player went up a ladder from y 51.3 to 63.4 at about (-2581, -236); [Move|Follow] route=partial, "standing still 3s - stepping back to the side", hops, "taking the way round ... doesn't end up at the target's height", never higher than 52.3. Built ladders aren't on the NavMesh, and FindClimb refuses a non-terrain rise > 1 m.
- New MHR_Mover.TryStartLadderClimb (with the use-ladder search when the route is partial and the goal > 2 m up, every 2 s; and at stuck step 0): within 14 m, a collider whose object/piece name contains "ladder", bottom within 1.5 m of our height, top at least 2 m above; nearest wins, each once per goal. Starts the existing Climb in ladder mode: ClimbDir = straight at the ladder (fixed), ClimbTop = ladder centre at min(top, needed height + 0.5); Climb keeps pushing ClimbDir (not the "reached the climb spot" sideways check), jumps when blocked (the existing jump-when-slow rule), rests when out of stamina, gives up after 5 s without 0.3 m gain; at the top a small leap forward to step off. Reset keeps a ladder climb going for the same tag while the goal is still above (moving player). If no ladder by that name is found, a Debug line lists the tall pieces close by (every 30 s) so the real name can be added.
- User: "the player just pushes into a ladder and that makes them auto jump up it. Does the NPC need to jump?" Checked the game: ladders carry an AutoJumpLedge trigger - OnTriggerStay calls Character.OnAutoJump(ledge forward, up/forward speed) for ANY Character (owner, on ground, not attacking/dodging, every 0.5 s) moving within 60 deg of the ledge's forward (Dot(m_moveDir, dir) >= 0.5). So no jumping needed, just walking into it the right way. Ladder climb now: a ladder is also recognised by an AutoJumpLedge in it (not just the name); with one, ClimbDir = the ledge's forward, ClimbBase = the foot on the side it's climbed from (centre - forward x (depth + 0.7)); Climb walks to the foot first, then pushes along ClimbDir and does no jumps of its own while the auto-jump is lifting it (it helps out only after 2 s with no height gain). Ladders without a ledge keep the push-and-hop.
- Ladder stops short (user: "If a ladder does not fully connect can the NPC jump up onto something nearby to finish getting to the destination?"): at the top of a ladder climb, LadderTopHop looks along the way to the goal (GoodPos if close and not below, else the goal), then along the ladder's direction, 0.8-3 m out in 0.4 m steps: footing from a downward ray between 0.5 m below and a jump's height above (jumpForce^2 / 2g x 0.85), 1.6 m headroom, within MHR_Jump.Reach for that rise -> MHR_Jump.Leap onto it ("jumping from the top of the ladder onto ..."). Nothing found: steps off along the ladder as before; the normal stuck steps (hops toward the target) still follow.
- User test (log 1afe6d51): "the NPC is using the ladder now. The top of this one ends in a wall and you have to jump on the wall". The ladder is "wood_stepladder" (name match worked); three stacked ladders 52.3 -> 56.3 -> 60.3, each "climbing the ladder" + "jumping from the top of the ladder onto ..." + "climbed the ladder". Then at 61.4, 2.0 m below the floor at 63.4: "no ladder found (tall pieces close by: wood_roof, wood_roof_top, woodwall, portal_wood, wood_floor ...)", standing still, "stepping back to the side" -> fell down the shaft to 56.1 and started over.
- TryHopUp (stuck step 0 after the ladder searches, and from LadderTopHop when no level footing): goal (or GoodPos) 0.5-6 m above and within 15 m flat; 12 directions (30 deg apart, starting at the goal), 0.6-2.6 m out: nearest footing that way 0.4 m above us to a jump's height (jumpForce^2/2g x 0.85), surface normal.y >= 0.6, 1.2 m headroom, within MHR_Jump.Reach; picks the highest (ties: closest to the goal's direction); MHR_Jump.Leap. Up to 6 hops per goal, so wall top -> floor is two. Sidestep now skips directions with a drop ahead (SafeClear) - it stepped back off the ladder top before.
- User test (log 5059df13): "There are 2 ladders to climb. One even I get stuck on ... The other works ... the NPC was having issues with finding the ladder and then picking the right path. It was running into a wall". Log: picked wood_stepladder at (-2579.9, 52.3, -236.2) 5.4 m away (the short/bad one), then "something in the way at (-2579.3, 52.3, -238.7)", standing still / no closer for 11 s (Climb steered straight at the ladder foot, through a wall); later climbed it to 52.9 only and then went for the one at (-2582.9, 52.3, -235.8) (the good one, used in log 1afe6d51).
- Fixes: ladder choice score = flat distance + 3 x (needed height - ladder top), so the one reaching highest wins; s_badLadders (static, by instance id) skips a ladder for 300 s after "couldn't get up" or a climb that gained < 1.5 m, 120 s after the foot couldn't be reached. With an AutoJumpLedge ladder more than 1 m away, the foot (ClimbBase) is reached as a routed detour first (LadderPending, 15 s): "heading for the ladder ..., foot at", then "at the foot of the ladder - climbing". Reset keeps a pending approach like a climb in progress.
- Going down (user: "what about going down a ladder? Will the NPC turn around if it gets stuck and jump as long as the fall distance is safe? Or jump down to lower ledge? ... a player will jump even if its not safe as long as its not a fall that will kill them ... they will of course go another way if its safer and not to far out of the way"). Game check: Character fall damage is IsPlayer() only (> 4 m: (h-4)/16 x 100) - NPCs never take fall damage. Built ladders only lift (AutoJumpLedge), there's no climbing down; players drop or take another way. New TryDropDown (stuck step 0, after the ladder/hop-up checks, goal >= 1.5 m below): 16 directions, 0.6-3 m out, first ground below between 1.2 m and [Movement] StuckDrop (8 m, 3-20), no deep water, not more than 4 m further from the goal flat; best = closest to the goal's height (then nearer, then smaller drop), and it must get at least 1 m closer to the goal's level; walks/steps off the edge. Only when stuck - routes (stairs, the way round) and the 3 m MaxDrop shortcuts come first.
- User: "The NPC should prefer to go back down the ladder. Also why do they not take fall damage. They should take fall damage just like a player, and try to avoid falling that far if they can." Patches/CompanionFallDamagePatch.cs: prefix/postfix on Character.UpdateGroundContact for companions (not players): fall = m_maxAirAltitude - y when touching ground; > 4 m -> (fall-4)/16 x 100 damage, SEMan.ModifyFallDamage (Feather Fall), HitType.Fall, owner only, not in liquid; logs "fell N m and took N fall damage". A move of > 6 m between two physics steps counts as a warp (portal, follow teleport, respawn, ladder use) and resets m_maxAirAltitude so it isn't a "fall". SafeDrop(c, share) = 4 + 16 x clamp01(health x share / 100). TryDropDown now caps at min(StuckDrop, SafeDrop(0.3)).
- TryLadderDown (stuck step 0 before TryDropDown; and every 2 s when the route is partial and the goal > 2 m below): a ladder (name or AutoJumpLedge) within 14 m whose top is on our level (-1.5..+1.2 m) and whose foot is nearer the goal's level; top spot = centre + forward x (depth + 0.6) at our height (where it's stepped off), foot = centre - forward x (depth + 0.7). Routed detour to the top spot (LadderPending + DescendLadder, 15 s), then Descend (10 s): walk towards the foot and let it carry us down; done at foot height + 1 m ("went down the ladder"); failing marks the ladder bad for 300 s. Reset keeps a descent/approach while the goal is still below.
- User log (2026-10-12, outpost, "Still having some issues"): every ladder climb ended ~1.5 m up ("climbed the ladder" at foot + 1.5), the top-hop then dropped him back to the ground, and the short rise marked the ladder bad (300 s) so the next platform logged "no ladder found" with a wood_stepladder close by. Two ladder feet at the same x/z 4 m apart (51.2, 55.2) = stacked pieces read as separate short ladders. He also shuffled round the first ladder's foot for 25 s (within ~0.85 m of the foot spot, walk-to-foot kept resetting the stuck clock). A command move never counted as "at the foot" (foot y = ladder bottom 53.2, ground 51.3).
- Fix: LadderBounds = a piece's colliders plus its AutoJumpLedge trigger, joined with ladder pieces stacked above/below (centre within 0.8 m flat, ends meeting within 1.5/0.8 m); the bottom piece's id is the ladder's id (tried/bad memory). Foot spot y = ground under it; a ladder may start up to 2.2 m above us. Climb: off the top only after 0.8 s with no lift (it bounces every 0.5 s); walk to the foot only if > 1.5 m off (> 0.6 m in the first 3 s), stuck clock held only for the first 8 s; "barely got anywhere" now < 1 m. Log shows the ladder's top height. Also in the log: one 4.3 m fall (2 damage) walking off a platform edge after the player moved - left as is.
- User: "The NPC should be able to jump onto the bottom of a ladder if they need. I do that all the time rather than trying to run up from the bottom. Not always but if I need to do it." Climb (auto ladders): S.ClimbBottomY = ladder bottom (stack); at the foot (not walking to it) jump at once when the bottom is > 0.4 m above us, else after 1 s of pushing without a lift (was 2 s and the generic pit-climb jump); c.Jump + 3 m/s along the ladder's direction, every 0.8 s at most; Dbg "jumping onto the ladder" (10 s throttle).
- User log ("Still getting stuck", 2026-10-12): the ladder at (-2581.7, -234.6) has its bottom 1.9 m above the ground (53.2 vs 51.3) and goes to 55.4; it scored best (top highest). He jumped at it every 0.8 s for 20+ s, never got on, stamina fell to 15% (no food eaten) and the climb's stamina rest held him at the foot; each jump's height reset the 5 s "no higher" clock, so he never gave up. Also: the ladder pieces here are ~2.2 m tall (51.2-53.4, 55.2-57.4 in one column, a floor at ~54 between) - not a 4 m stack, so the stack join correctly doesn't merge them.
- Fix: jump-on tries limited to 4 (LadderJumpTries) while not yet 0.8 m above the climb start, 1.2 s apart; then "couldn't get onto the ladder ... after N jumps (its bottom is X m up, best Y m) - trying another way", ladder marked bad 120 s, stuck clock primed so the next options (other ladder, hop-up) run. Each jump is logged with its try number. Ladder score adds 5 per metre the bottom is above us (past 0.5 m) so one that reaches the ground wins when the tops are close.
- User: "the NPC was standing under the small floor section between the 2 ladders and just jumping in place ... kept hitting the floor tile just above their head." The 1.9 m-up ladder starts from that floor; its foot spot (0.7 m out) missed the floor edge, so the ground under it was used. New LadderFoot(bounds, forward): first a floor at about the ladder's bottom (ray from bottom+0.6 down 1.2 m) at 0.7/0.4/1.0/1.3 m in front of it, else the ground below; room = nothing within 2 m above the spot or 0.3 m further in (ladder pieces ignored). TryStartLadderClimb skips a ladder whose foot is > 1 m above us (climbed from a floor we have to reach first) or has no room; the foot spot is the climb base.
- User log + "Near the end of the log I climbed up the ladders to the top. Thats the path the NPC should follow ... It should look for a path to where it needs to go, or at least close. Picking a ladder near it feet that it can ether walk up or jump onto. Ignore ladders that cause it to get stuck." The player's route (follow goals): ground -> ~53.4 floor -> 56 (-2579.6, -237.0) -> 58.7 -> 60.1 -> 63 (-2580.6, -234.7): a chain of ~2.2 m ladder pieces with small floors between. Log: Gunter climbed (-2584.8, -236.6) five times; each time on the 53.4 floor "no ladder found" - the filter wanted a ladder top >= 2 m above us, and the next piece (53.2-55.4) only rises 2.0 m from that floor; then unstuck steps walked/stepped him off the floor back to the ground.
- Fix: TryStartLadderClimb candidates: top > 1 m above us; with an AutoJumpLedge: LadderFoot spot within 1 m of our height, a jump-on (bottom > 0.4 m over the spot) needs room and <= 2 m; walk = flat distance (<= 3 m) else a route to the foot that ends within 1 m (else skipped) and its length. Score = walk + flat(top, goal) + 2 x |needY - top| + 2 per m of jump-on. Without a ledge: old reach rule + same goal term. NoteLadderClimb: the same ladder started 3 times in 120 s -> bad for 180 s ("keeps ending up back where it started"). After a ladder climb the next ladder search runs at once.
- User log ("Not yet."): chain is ground -> (-2584.8 or -2579.9) -> 53.4 floor -> (-2580.9, -236.6) 53.2-55.4 -> 55.4 floor -> (-2579.1, -238.2) 55.3-57.4 -> 57.4 floor -> (-2581.7, -234.6) 57.3-59.4 -> ... 63. He reached 56.5 once. Failures were all falls: the routed approach to the next foot (1.2 m away) walked off the small floor (fell 4.1 m); after a fall the climb carried on and jumped at the ladder from the ground under its floor, then "couldn't get up" marked a good ladder bad, so the next pass on the 53.4 floor found "no ladder"; the 3-uses guard then blocked the ground ladder.
- Fix: a foot within 3 m flat and 1 m height is walked to by Climb directly (no route); walking to the foot, a drop 0.6 m ahead (no ground within 1.6 m below) ends the attempt ("past a drop - trying another", ladder only in the tried set). Climb: below the start height by > 1 m on the ground = fell off -> stop without marking the ladder, search again at once. Routed approach: below the foot by > 1.5 m = fell off, same. Repeat guard now 4 starts in 120 s.
- User: "Does the wander logic conflict with climbing a ladder?" Yes - not IdleController (off while following) but MHR_Follow's own stop/idle: with the player stopped, distP (3D when dy > 3) <= FollowMaxDistance (10, indoors 4) counted as arrived even a floor or two down the tower -> StopMoving mid-ladder, then the Wander shuffle sent Go an IdlePoint at the player's height, replacing the ladder plan. Fix: offLevel (|dy| > 2.5, nobody swimming) and MHR_Mover.OnTheWayUp (climb/ladder approach/descent/hop under way) count as "too far": keep moving to a spot by the player; while moving, they start movement even inside the hold. Gave up while close but off-level: stand and wait (not the vanilla Follow fallback, which pushes at the wall below the player).
- User log + "We dont seem to be making much progress. Its a narrow small space." He reached 57.4 several times; the top-hop there (0.8 m on) took him off the floor: "fell 7.8 m and took 23 fall damage"; the next foot was "past a drop"; then "no ladder found" again. Shape guessing doesn't work in that tower.
- New approach - MHR_Follow.WalkPlayerPath: T.Steps records the player every 0.35 m in 3D (400 max, cleared on teleport). When the companion is off the player's level (> 2.5 m) and the mover's route is partial / gave up / is on a ladder: find the LAST step within 0.5 m of our height and 12 m flat (where the player last stood on our floor), walk there with the mover, then steer step to step directly (MoveTowards, facing the step - the same direction into each ladder, which is what makes AutoJumpLedge lift), passing a step at < 0.5 m flat (and not > 1 m below it), skipping ahead up to 10 steps if lifted past them; jump when the next step is > 0.6 m up and 0.5 s without progress (1 s apart). Ends on the player's level; "fell off your path" (> 2.5 m below the step) -> retry after 3 s; no progress 6 s -> "stuck on your path", off 15 s; approach not reached in 15 s -> off 10 s. Logs "[Follow] ... following your path (N steps, starting X m away)" and a debug line every 3 s with the step index. Mover ladder logic stays as the fallback.
- User log ("It did finally make it up the top. It still had a very hard time ... As it might need to use pathing to get to an enemy."): no "[Follow] ... following your path" line at all - either the DLL predates e0deb0a or there were no steps on its level (now logged: "can't follow your path: none of your last N steps ..."). Mover only: two falls off the 57.4 floor right after climbing to it (7.6 m / 22 dmg, 6.3 m / 14 dmg) walking a route the navmesh called "full"; at the ground ladder (-2579.9, -236.2) ~20 s of "jumping onto the ladder" once 1.2 m up (the 4-try limit only counted jumps from below).
- Fix: MHR_Mover.LedgeAhead - 0.7 m ahead no ground within MaxDrop (3 m) and the ground below isn't >= 1 m of water -> Halt, Dbg "stopping at the edge ... (N m down)", stuck steps after StillTime (ladder down, TryDropDown capped by SafeDrop, other ways). In GoInner's normal steering (after water/gap/fire), so Follow, combat ChaseMove, HuntMove and jobs all get it; deliberate drops/hops (StepUntil), ladder descents and climbs run before it. Ladder helper jumps: counted whether below or part way up, reset by a new best height; 4 without one -> "couldn't get further up / onto the ladder", bad 120 s.
- User: "The edge guard only applied to drops? Not to jumping gaps?" - right: GapAhead runs before LedgeAhead. Now GapAhead uses 80% of MHR_Jump.Reach when LedgeAhead (0.6 m) says a miss would be a harmful dry fall; LadderTopHop's fallback step-off halts instead over such a ledge (Hop already sidesteps on DropAhead). Drop-down when stuck stays TryDropDown <= min(StuckDrop 8, SafeDrop 30%) - a setting for the 30% was offered, not asked for.
- User log ("Its a bit better. Let me know if you find any more things to adjust"): path-walk ran (up and down), the edge guard stopped him at 3.6-12.5 m drops, no fall damage, Command went down the ladders. Smoothing: path-walk stopped silently each time the height difference crossed 2.5 m and the mover's ladder search took over in between -> on the path it now keeps going until within 1.2 m of the player's height. Path-walk had no drop check -> before stepping on, open air below 0.7 m ahead deeper than SafeDrop(30%) (a ladder's rungs or a floor below are fine) ends it ("you went down a drop of more than N m there - too far for me", off 15 s; the mover's ladder-down/drop logic takes over). Log fixes: "N m above/below you" was signed; "climbing the ladder ... (N m away)" printed the score.
- User test (51cc976): "Much better this time around. Still had a little bit of an issue getting up on the final floor. That does not matter as much. Most players will not build such a janky outpost." Log: path-walk up and down; refused the player's 4.6 m+ jump down ("too far for me") and took three ladders down instead; edge guard stops at 3.1-6.1 m; no fall damage. Last floor: ladder at (-2580.7, 62.3, -234.4) + 0.5 m top hop - slow but made it. Ladders considered done for 1.5.2.
- BACKLOG (user, 2026-10-12): ladder handling should work for every movement, not just Follow - "it might need to use pathing to get to an enemy" (an archer on a tower). The player-path walk is Follow-only; combat/jobs rely on the mover's ladder search. Idea: remember ladder chains a companion (or the player) has climbed successfully (foot -> top pairs per base) and let the planner use them as links; revisit with the combat overhaul.
- BACKLOG (user): mining high up on an ore vein - breaking the part you stand on drops you; a player accepts that with some food in and a short drop. Revisit gather/mining positioning now that fall damage is on (prefer spots it won't fall far from; fine if the drop is small and health is good).
- Applies to every MHR_Mover user: Follow, combat chases (ChaseMove), hunting (HuntMove) and jobs.
- User also: "The NPC did empty their bag this time when I loaded the game back up" - log shows "[Tools] Back home - will empty bags" and the put-aways (BjornHide, BjornPaw, DeerHide, BjornMeat, DeerMeat, TrophyDeer, Resin...). Working.
- Correction (user): the odd water jump was just before the portal, not in the deer chase. Log: [Move|Follow] "jumping as far across the water as it can (lands at (2460.0, 30.0, 1990.3))", then "taking the 10m way round" and "jumping the shallow water at (2460.3, 29.8, 1991.0)" - the moving player changed the route and it jumped straight back. MHR_Mover water jumps now record the direction/time; a jump within 6 s pointing back (dot < -0.3) is refused (wade or route instead).
- Same stretch: ~40 "[Combat] Engage -> $enemy_crocodile ... its target=Gunter dist=9.5m" in a row, standing still, until the portal. The crocodile was in deep water and after Gunter: Engage -> UpdateCombat -> AvoidChase (would swim, no ranged weapon) -> ignore 20 s + disengage -> next tick FindEnemy/the vanilla-target fallback engaged it again (IsPassive skips the water-ignore when the creature is coming). New MHR_Threat.IgnoredInWater (ignore timer running and still in water > 1 m deep, coming or not) skips it for starting a fight on its own (both the FindEnemy path and the m_targetCreature fallback, which now also respects IsPassive). Being hit is still answered (OnDamaged).
- Ladders (user: second video - a skeleton up a Black Forest tower, the companion "just running around underneath the mob. It needed to go up a nearby ladder"): vanilla Ladder is an Interactable that moves the user to m_targetPos when within m_useDistance (2 m) - no climbing. MHR_Mover: TryLadder (when the main route is partial and the goal is > 2 m up, every 2 s; and first at stuck step 0, before climbing out) picks a Ladder within 25 m flat, bottom within 3 m of our height, top at least 1.5 m above us and > 2 m closer to the goal (score = walk + remaining); walks to it as a detour (20 s) and UseLadderNow calls Ladder.Interact(humanoid) once within reach, then replans from the top. Each ladder once per goal (LaddersUsed). Reset keeps a ladder in progress for the same tag while the goal is still above (a moving target). Works for every MHR_Mover user (combat chase, jobs, follow). Logs: "going up the ladder at", "went up the ladder to". Not handled: coming back down (drops/jumps as before).
- CORRECTION (user): the ladders in the video are NOT the Ladder component - Valheim's wooden ladders (towers included) are stair-like, the player hops up them without using anything. So the TryLadder work above probably doesn't fire there (harmless; kept for the use-to-climb kind). Likely real cause: NavMesh routes stop at the ladder foot (too steep), and climb-out (FindClimb) refuses non-terrain rises > 1 m, so nothing goes up. Planned fix: recognise a ladder near where it's stuck (collider/prefab name - waiting on the user for the hover name and a Debug log reproducing it) and hop up it with the existing Climb routine. Guide line removed, changelog line narrowed.
- Combat breakdown for the overhaul was given in the chat (stances, weapon choice, blocking, flee, gaps) - not written here.

### 1.5.1 - bed claiming (user test, 2026-10-11: "The NPC kept reporting that it had no bed. I went to try and sleep and somehow claimed the unused bed and then had to rebuild the bed and the NPC still did not claim it.")
- Log had no [Rest] bed lines at all (no claim, no re-link, no "Going to bed").
- Bug 1: CompanionRest.Update's daytime bed search ran inside the once-a-second block but subtracted Time.deltaTime from the 30 s timer -> ~30 min between searches. Now -= 1f. (At night TryAutoSleep searches every 5 s, but only when it's trying to sleep: SleepAtNight on, night, no enemy within 15 m, not told to stay awake.)
- Bug 2: _needsBed (drives the "no bed" speech) was only cleared by the night pass - now also cleared on ClaimBed and the re-link.
- Stale claims: a bed whose HC claim ID belongs to a companion that isn't anywhere in the world (no companion ZDO, no respawning tombstone - MHR_Roster.CompanionGone, scan of ZDOMan.m_objectsByID cached 120 s, single player/host only) no longer counts as taken. Before, such a bed was blocked forever.
- Diagnostics: "[Rest] No bed to claim: <reason>" when the reason changes - no home and none of the player's beds within 20 m (the no-home rule needs a player-owned bed nearby), no bed within the radius, or N beds / slept in by a player / taken by another companion.
- User follow-up: "I had claimed the bed well after it was night ... Can we make an NPC use a bed that is claimed by their owner and not set as a spawn point? ... very easy to click on the bed and claim it ... It was like after recovering the NPC and loading up the game again it lost ownership of the bed."
- Cause of the lost bed: the first bed search ran the moment the companion loaded (_bedClaimScanTimer started at 0). ZNetScene builds at most 10 objects a frame near the player (m_maxCreatedPerFrame), so the bed often didn't exist yet -> FindAnyBedNear(bedPos, 2 m) found nothing -> HasHomeBed cleared for good, and without a home the 20 m "a player bed nearby" rule then blocked a new claim. Now: first search 5 s after load; a missing bed is kept for 60 s (search every 5 s meanwhile) before it's given up, with a log line.
- Owner's beds: CompanionRest.MHR_BedOwnerOk replaces the "bed owner == 0" checks (claim, re-link, keep, DirectSleep, FindUnclaimedBedNear, FindNearbyBed). OK when unowned, or owned by the companion's owner on this computer and not the profile's custom spawn point (Bed.GetSpawnPoint vs PlayerProfile.GetCustomSpawnPoint, < 1 m = spawn, like Bed.IsCurrent). When another player's game runs the companion it can't see the owner's spawn point, so it keeps a bed it already has (keeping: true) but doesn't take a new owner bed. Hover on an owned bed with a companion claim adds "(name sleeps here)".
- Hover (user, 2026-10-11: "just say The NPC sleeps here ... Just like we do on an unclaimed bed"): on an owned bed it uses, the first line (the player's name) becomes "<name>'s Bed"; the game's own lines (sleep / set spawn) stay.
- User test: re-link on load worked ("Re-linked to its claimed bed"), but the bed still said "(Gunter sleeps here)" after the user made it their spawn point. The hover only checked the claim name, and the companion re-checks its bed every 30 s (no release line in the log). Now the hover line needs the bed to be the local player's and not Bed.IsCurrent(), and a PlayerProfile.SetCustomSpawnPoint postfix (Patches/SpawnPointBedPatch.cs) zeroes every companion's bed-scan timer so it gives the bed up on its next tick.

### 1.5.1 - companion roster and recovery, step 1 (user, 2026-10-11: "We can have button that is in the config to recover a lost NPC ... if ever the player came upon that NPC again in the wild the found NPC that was replaced would get removed")
- User chose: roster + config recovery first, the Companion Shrine (buildable, cheap early-game recipe, vanilla stone/runestone model, list window Alive/Lost/Unknown, select + confirm) in the next update; Unknown companions recoverable with a warning.
- Companion/MHR_Roster.cs: Player.m_customData["HC_Roster_<worldUID>"], one base64 ZPackage per companion (version 1) with id, generation, name, prefab, appearance, owner, mode/schema, follow/followPaused/stayHome/autoPickup/wander/commandable/formation/combatStance, skills, home, bed, inventory (Inventory.Save), last position/time (ZNet time), dead flag. Snapshot every [Advanced] RosterSaveInterval (60 s) for live companions we own (ZDO owner + owner id = local player), saved to disk only on recovery/despawn (the game saves the profile on its own).
- Generation: companion ZDO "HC_Gen", tombstone "HC_Respawn_Gen" (stamped in CreateCompanionTombstone, copied to the respawned companion in MHR_ResurrectCore). Recovery spawns gen+1 and bumps the roster.
- Stale copy (gen < roster gen) found loaded and ours: removed (CompanionSetup._mhrStale lets it past MHR_DeleteGuard, PrepareForDespawn for the pin), message + log. Stale tombstone: MHR_ResurrectCore refuses, clears its respawn data, items stay.
- Status: Alive if loaded; on single player/host a scan of ZDOMan.m_objectsByID (AccessTools) finds the companion (Alive), a tombstone with respawn data (Dead) or nothing (Lost). A client of a dedicated server only holds nearby ZDOs -> Unknown (shows "last seen"/"last known: died" near x, z).
- [General] ShowCompanionRoster (list, centre message + log) and RecoverLostCompanion (first Lost; else first Unknown needs a second press within 60 s).
