## 1.4.20 — found and fixed a real conflict for Ranged's ammo check; the log couldn't yet prove it, so added direct logging; noted (not yet diagnosed) a "stuck in passive" + water-crossing report
User's 1.4.19 test: the 15m `BowSwitchDistance` works properly now (Balanced switches at the right range). Ranged stance was shooting anything aggro'd - but the arrow check "does not seem to work" - the companion wouldn't switch away from the bow even with no arrows. Separately: after dying and crossing water, the companion "seemed to get stuck in passive mode again," and stayed that way through a full timeout wait.
- **Checked the log first, honestly: it doesn't actually contain a confirmed zero-ammo moment.** Only one `EQUIPDIAG` line traces back to `TryForceRangedWeapon` this whole session, and it succeeded (found the bow, tried to equip - `EquipItem($item_bow_finewood) -> FALSE` just means it was already equipped, not an error). One `EquipItem($item_arrow_wood) -> true` shows ammo actually was available at some point. There's no log evidence either way for what happened once arrows ran out - `TryForceRangedWeapon`/`FindReadyBow` had zero logging of their own (a repeat of the same visibility gap that bit the Balanced-distance investigation), so this report currently can't be confirmed OR ruled out from the log alone.
- **While tracing the code path to add that logging, found a real, confirmed bug regardless:** `Companions.CombatPatches/EquipBestWeapon_PreferHighestDamage.Postfix` (the old pre-Balanced "prefer highest effective damage" weapon-picker, still Harmony-patched onto `Humanoid.EquipBestWeapon`) only ever skipped itself for Balanced-stance companions (`IsBalancedCompanion(h)`). For a Ranged or Melee companion it was still running every single `EquipBestWeapon` call, scoring every usable weapon by raw `WeaponLogic.Score` - **with no ammo check at all** - and re-equipping whatever scored highest. Since a bow typically scores well on raw damage, this postfix could re-equip a dry bow completely independent of stance intent, immediately adjacent to (and racing) `TryForceRangedWeapon`'s own deliberate, ammo-aware choice on the very same decision cycle. This is a real conflict between two systems fighting over the same weapon slot, whether or not it's the exact mechanism behind tonight's specific report.
- **Fixed**: replaced the guard with a new `WeaponLogic.IsManagedStance(Humanoid)` (Balanced=0, Ranged=2, Melee=3 - all three now have their own dedicated, deliberate weapon-selection logic: `BalancedPrefix`, `TryForceRangedWeapon`→`FindReadyBow`, `TryForceMeleeWeapon` respectively). Only Passive (stance=1, which has no dedicated weapon logic and shouldn't be fighting in the first place) still falls through to the old damage-based postfix.
- **Added direct logging to `FindReadyBow`** (rate-limited to 40): every call now logs one of four outcomes - a ready bow found and about to be equipped; no bow because ammo is missing (names the bow and the exact ammo type it needs); a bow exists but isn't usable (durability/equipable); or no bow-type item in inventory at all. This turns "does the arrow check work" into something the next log will state directly instead of requiring inference from indirect `EQUIPDIAG` traces.
- Both changes are in `WeaponLogic`, rebuilt as a full-class-body replacement again. The `IsManagedStance` call-site swap in the old Postfix (renaming which static method the existing `brfalse.s notBalanced / ret` branch calls) is a same-shape, one-line substitution - no label or branch structure changed. `ilasm` reassembled clean.
- **"Stuck in passive mode" after death + crossing water: not investigated yet, and this log doesn't have enough to go on.** Checked: `ApplyFollowMode` shows `mode=0 stayHome=False follow=True` throughout (that's the separate follow/stay-home system, not a combat stance, and it looks normal). Zero `WaterSafety` log lines the whole session despite the user saying they crossed water, meaning either the companion's (custom) stamina never actually dropped low enough while swimming to trigger that system, or it didn't need to invoke it. The `[Balanced] skip: stance=2` line confirms the companion was in Ranged stance the whole time recorded, not Passive, at least at that logging point - but the death/respawn happened early in this log (companion fully re-`Awake()`s on respawn, a normal reload, not obviously related to any stance value), and there's no log line anywhere showing a stance actually change to Passive (stance=1) or get stuck there. Given how thin the evidence is, this needs its own dedicated test: reproduce the "stuck in passive" moment specifically (what does it look like in-game - won't follow? won't fight? radial menu shows Passive selected when it shouldn't be?) and grab a log bracketing just that incident, ideally without the arrow-check test mixed into the same session so the log stays easy to read for each issue.
- Build tag `v38-ranged-postfix-guard-log`, version 1.4.20 (length-6 version string, same-length byte edit, decoded and confirmed: `id=com.profmags.companions`, `name=Offline Companions`, `version=1.4.20`, trailer `00 00`).

### Next steps
- Test Ranged stance again, specifically watching for the moment arrows hit zero, and send that log - the new `[Ranged] FindReadyBow -> ...` lines will say directly whether it detected no ammo and whether the melee fallback was supposed to fire. If it still doesn't visibly switch weapons despite the log showing `-> none - ... -> falling back to melee`, that's the sign `TryForceMeleeWeapon` itself (already-existing code, unmodified this round) has its own problem, separate from the ammo detection.
- For "stuck in passive": next time it happens, note roughly what was happening right before (had the companion just been in combat? was the player far away? just after a respawn/reload?) and grab a log covering that window specifically.

## 1.4.21 — "companion stops reacting to everything" traced and ruled out for two suspects; rebuilt "come to me" as a distance-based return instead of the old (deadlock-prone) recall timer; added tick-level AI diagnostics
User's latest report reframed the earlier "stuck in passive" finding: it's not a stance issue at all - after the player dies, the companion sometimes stops reacting to *anything* (won't fight, won't switch weapons on stance/ammo changes) while still following normally. Investigated two leading theories against the actual uploaded log before touching any code:
- **`CompanionCombatAI.Recall()` deadlock - real bug, but not the cause of this specific incident.** Traced the full mechanism: `Recall()` sets `_recallTimer = 8f` and `CompanionAI.UpdateAI` refuses to call `FindEnemy()`/`Engage()` at all while `IsRecalled` (`_recallTimer > 0`) is true - but `_recallTimer` is only ever decremented inside `UpdateCombat()`, which itself only runs once already `IsEngaged`. Calling `Recall()` while *not* already mid-fight is therefore a permanent deadlock: nothing can ever clear it. `Recall()` is only reachable from `CompanionInteract.FireComeToMe`, which logs `"[Direct] Come-to-me triggered"` on every call - that string appears in **zero** of the user's five uploaded logs, including the latest one, so this specific freeze was not caused by an accidental "come to me" trigger (the user doesn't have it keybound and doesn't recall pressing it). Confirmed real bug, just not this incident.
- **`WaterSafety.Tick`'s tick-hijacking - also ruled out.** This existing system fully takes over `CompanionAI.UpdateAI` for a tick while a companion is swimming with low stamina and heading for shore (and `UpdateAI` returns immediately without reaching combat logic at all while it's active). It logs on activation (`"[WaterSafety] ..."`, confirmed present in an older log from a session when it worked normally) - zero matches in the latest log, so it never activated this time.
- **`CompanionAI.UpdateGroundStuckRecovery` - checked and cleared.** Another tick-hijacking system (stuck-on-terrain recovery). Fully traced: self-terminating by design (2.5s per attempt, max 5 attempts, then either teleports or backs off for 30s) - can't produce an indefinite freeze.
- **Root cause not yet confirmed.** Rather than keep guessing at `CompanionAI.UpdateAI`'s ~10 early-exit gates one at a time, added direct diagnostic logging to all of them (see below) so the *next* occurrence identifies the actual culprit from the log directly.

Two things shipped this round:

**1. `_recallTimer`/`IsRecalled` deadlock removed at its root, and the "come to me" feature rebuilt as the user specified, independent of whether it was this incident's cause:**
- `CompanionAI.UpdateAI`'s combat gate no longer reads `IsRecalled` at all - collapsed to a constant `false` at the one call site (`get_IsRecalled()` itself is left in place, just no longer consulted here). This means nothing can ever block engagement/self-defense again, regardless of what else calls `Recall()` in the future.
- New `Companions.ReturnLogic` static class (external, reflection-based, same pattern as the existing `WaterSafety`/`ShieldLogic` helpers) replaces the old mechanism:
  - **`Trigger(setup, playerGO)`** - forces `Follow` on (remembering the previous value to restore later), re-links the follow target to the player, and if the companion is currently engaged, checks the distance to its target: if nothing is within 4m (melee range) right now, calls `Disengage()` to break off immediately; otherwise leaves the fight alone (finishes naturally). Self-defense is never blocked - this never gates `FindEnemy`/`Engage`, only ever pulls toward the player when otherwise idle (via the existing, already-working follow system).
  - **`Tick(setup)`** - called once per companion per `UpdateAI` tick (cheap, no branching effect on the caller). Once the companion is back within 18m of the player (the same distance already used for the bow-switch tuning), clears the return state and restores whatever `Follow` setting it had before - so a Stay/Home companion goes back to staying/working once you're close again instead of being stuck in Follow.
  - **`OnPlayerRespawn()`** - new call added to the existing `PlayerHooks.Postfix` (the Harmony postfix on `Player.OnSpawned`, which already fires on every respawn to relink follow targets - confirmed via IL trace, not a guess). Loops the local player's own companions (ownership-filtered via the existing `CompanionInteract.IsOwned`) and calls `Trigger` on each, exactly like the manual key does.
  - `CompanionInteract.FireComeToMe`'s single `Recall()` call site now calls `ReturnLogic.Trigger` instead.
  - **Not done this round**: a separate, initially-unbound keybind for a manual "return to me" command distinct from the existing `DirectTargetKey` (Z by default) - the auto-trigger-on-death path is wired up and doesn't need one, and `DirectTargetKey` already exists as a manual trigger. Say if a *second*, dedicated key is still wanted alongside it.
- Hardcoded constants for now: `CloseRange = 4f`, `ReturnDistance = 18f` (not yet exposed as BepInEx config entries, to keep this build's surface area smaller - can be promoted to config if you want them tunable without a rebuild).

**2. `Companions.AIGateDiag` - new diagnostic-only class.** Logs which of `CompanionAI.UpdateAI`'s early-exit gates is holding a companion's tick (vanilla `BaseAI.UpdateAI()` returning false, `WaterSafety`, `Sleeping`, `FreezeTimer`, `Resting`, `OnShip`, UI-open/panel-or-radial, `HazardRecovery`), plus a "resumed normal AI" line once nothing is blocking. Only logs on a state change (gate starts/stops) plus a heartbeat every 15s while a gate stays blocked, rate-limited to 200 total lines - won't spam a normal session. If the non-reactivity bug recurs, the log will now say directly (`[AIGate] "<name>" blocked by <gate>`) which system is responsible instead of requiring another round of guessing.

- Both `ReturnLogic` and `AIGateDiag` compiled via `mcs` from `Companions_helpers.cs` (same toolchain as `WeaponLogic`/`ShieldLogic`/`WaterSafety` this session) and spliced in as full class bodies - the established low-risk pattern. The `UpdateAI` insertions (gate diagnostics + the `IsRecalled` collapse + the `ReturnLogic.Tick` call) were all straight-line, non-branching additions between existing labeled instructions, verified label-safe before editing (checked no other branch in the method targets any label being touched). The `FireComeToMe`/`PlayerHooks.Postfix` edits are small, targeted call-site swaps, same as prior sessions' style. `ilasm` reassembled clean on the first attempt.
- Build tag `v39-return-logic-aigate-diag`, version 1.4.21 (length-6 version string, same-length byte edit, decoded and confirmed: `id=com.profmags.companions`, `name=Offline Companions`, `version=1.4.21`, trailer `00 00`). Also fixed the long-standing cosmetic mismatch where the "Offline Companions vX.Y.Z loading..." log line was hardcoded to `v1.4.16` regardless of actual version - it now reads `v1.4.21`.

### Next steps
- Test as before (stance/ammo changes, combat). If the "stops reacting to everything" freeze happens again, the log will now show either an `[AIGate] ... blocked by ...` line naming the real cause, or (if it doesn't appear at all) that `UpdateAI` itself isn't even being reached for that companion - which would point at something upstream (e.g. network ownership) rather than anything inside this method.
- Specifically test dying near a companion and confirm it comes back to you and resumes normal behavior (fighting, stance changes) once close again - this exercises the new `ReturnLogic` path directly.
- If a second, dedicated "return to me" keybind (distinct from the existing `DirectTargetKey`) is still wanted, say so and it can be added as its own config entry defaulting to unbound.
- `CloseRange`/`ReturnDistance` are hardcoded (4f/18f) - can be promoted to config entries if you want to tune them without a rebuild.
- Roadmap items not yet started: Passive-stance flee-to-player-if-following/flee-to-home-if-working; night light while foraging/gathering after dark; the 27 dead config entries; the optional distance-based temporary melee fallback for Ranged stance.
- Previously-agreed, not recently re-verified: torch opener should pick the *best* torch among all usable ones, not just the first found.
- Deferred: kiting large monsters; chest-inventory visual-overlay cosmetic bug (no repro); the "server only" architecture question; a future "magic bar" to replace the weight bar.
- User-owned: checking for an F8-keybind conflict with a controller-remap mod; testing with a physical controller.

## 1.4.22 — 1.4.21's new diagnostics ruled out the early-exit gates entirely; added one more targeted log for the Passive-stance skip
User tested 1.4.21 and reported the freeze happened again - this time without dying, confirming it's not death/respawn-specific at all.
- **The new `[AIGate]` logging did its job**: across the whole log, the only gate that ever fired was "UI open (panel/radial)" - correctly, right when the player opened/closed the companion radial menu (each one resumed normal AI immediately after, as expected). None of the other 7 instrumented gates (vanilla `BaseAI.UpdateAI()` false, `WaterSafety`, `Sleeping`, `FreezeTimer`, `Resting`, `OnShip`, `HazardRecovery`) ever blocked. This rules out that entire category of causes with actual evidence, not just for this incident but as a general statement about how the companion behaved the whole session.
- **Found a real gap in the diagnostics, not yet in the mechanism**: `AIGateDiag`'s instrumentation only covered `UpdateAI`'s *early* exit gates - it didn't cover the Passive-stance skip further down in the same method (right where the old `IsRecalled` deadlock check used to live, now permanently disabled). If the companion's stance was actually Passive at the time - even briefly, even unintentionally - `UpdateAI` would report "resumed normal AI" (since it passed all the early gates) while silently skipping `FindEnemy()`/`Engage()` a few lines later, with zero log trace. That would look exactly like this bug and wasn't being caught.
- **Fixed the gap**: added one more `AIGateDiag.Report(setup, "Passive stance")` call at that exact skip point (a single, non-branching insertion right after the existing label, no logic changed - the `IsRecalled` half of that same check is confirmed dead code now, since it's hardcoded to `false`, so this path is only ever reached by an actual Passive stance).
- Also checked the log's own combat-equip lines directly: zero `[Balanced]`/`[Ranged]`/`[Shield]`/weapon-`EQUIPDIAG` activity in the final ~2 minutes of the session, right when several monsters (Greydwarf x2, Greyling, SA_GreenTurtle x2) spawned nearby - consistent with the companion just not fighting, but the log doesn't clearly capture the moment an enemy was actually attacking it, so this build's new log is needed to pin down whether Passive stance was the actual cause here.
- `ilasm` reassembled clean.
- Build tag `v40-passive-stance-aigate-diag`, version 1.4.22 (length-6 version string, same-length byte edit, decoded and confirmed: `id=com.profmags.companions`, `name=Offline Companions`, `version=1.4.22`, trailer `00 00`).

If NO gate fires at all (not even Passive) while the companion is still unresponsive, the cause is downstream of all of `UpdateAI`'s skip logic entirely - most likely `BaseAI.FindEnemy()` itself not finding the attacker (detection range/LOS) or `IsPassiveAnimal()` incorrectly filtering it out - and the next diagnostic pass will target those two specifically.

## 1.4.23 — 1.4.22's log ruled out the last new `[AIGate]` gate as benign; fixed a real bug where "return to me" never fired on an actual death (wrong Harmony hook); shield-logic investigation opened
User tested 1.4.22, reported "no issue this time," but three findings needed following up:
- **The one new `[AIGate] blocked by vanilla BaseAI.UpdateAI() false` this test hit - traced and explained.** It runs for ~100s starting right after the player's own death/respawn-at-bed (`"Starting respawn"` / `"Local player destroyed"` / `"Found bed at custom spawn point"`, all Unity/vanilla log lines, not this mod's), then self-clears. Not the companion's own death (which happened earlier in the same log and is a separate, already-understood system - `CompanionManager.DoRespawn`). No other gate fired anywhere else in the log except the expected UI-open ones, so nothing was actually stuck this session, consistent with "no issue."
- **Found a real bug: the auto "return to me" pull-in never fires on an actual death, only on the initial world-load spawn.** `[Return] triggered` appears exactly once in the whole log, at session start - not at the player's death/respawn. Root cause, confirmed by inspecting the actual game assembly directly (not guessed): `PlayerHooks.Postfix` is patched onto `Player.OnSpawned`, which only fires once per session (initial spawn). Valheim's `Player` class has a **separate** method, `OnRespawn()`, that's the real hook for a death-respawn - `OnSpawned` doesn't run again on death.
- **Fixed**: added a second Harmony postfix patch, `Companions.PlayerRespawnHooks` (`[HarmonyPatch(typeof(Player), "OnRespawn")]`), whose `Postfix()` calls a new `Companions.RespawnHooks.FullRestore()` - a straight port of what `PlayerHooks.Postfix` already does (loop `CompanionSetup.RestoreFollowTarget()` + `CompanionAI.IgnorePlayerCollision()` over all companions) plus the existing `ReturnLogic.OnPlayerRespawn()` pull-in. `PlayerHooks`/`Player.OnSpawned` is left exactly as-is (still needed for the initial world-load spawn) - this is purely additive, nothing removed. Compiled via `mcs` from `Companions_helpers.cs` (same toolchain as every other helper class this session) and spliced in as a full class body; the tiny hand-written `PlayerRespawnHooks` wrapper class reuses `PlayerHooks`'s exact `HarmonyPatch` custom-attribute byte blob with only the method-name bytes swapped (`"OnSpawned"` → `"OnRespawn"`, both 9 bytes, single-byte substitution, no length change - the pattern used for version bumps all session). `ilasm` reassembled clean; verified via `monodis` that both new classes exist in the output DLL and via UTF-16 string dump that the build tag/version strings updated correctly.
- **Shield-logic discrepancy (3 live `ShieldLogic` decisions, all round shield, vs. 4 raw tower-shield equips) - explained but not yet fixed.** Traced `ShieldLogic.SelectShield`: it only runs when the companion is currently holding a one-handed weapon (needs a free hand) and only gets called from `SelectBestAttack`, which itself only runs while there's a live `_targetCreature`/`_targetStatic` (i.e., only during active combat, gated by `_updateWeaponTimer`/`_weaponSwitchCooldown` throttling). So: (a) while the companion is holding the bow, shield selection never runs at all - whatever shield is already equipped (including one from a post-respawn default-gear re-equip, which may be the tower shield) just sits there untouched; (b) once combat ends, shield category is never re-evaluated until the *next* fight's first `SelectBestAttack` tick - so a tower shield picked during a low-HP moment can outlive that fight and look "stuck" even after healing, until something re-triggers a weapon-selection tick. This matches the user's newest report ("tower shield equipped most of the time," "some of it was low health") - the low-health -> tower/block swap itself (`hpPct < HealthRetreatPct` (default 30%) `|| nearbyAlertedEnemies >= ShieldMultiAttackerCount` (default 3)) is working as designed, but the *lack of any out-of-combat re-evaluation* likely makes it look stickier/more tower-biased than intended. Not yet fixed - needs a design discussion (see below) before touching the code, and ideally a fresh log from this build to confirm the theory against real equip/health timestamps rather than the mostly-inferred read on the 1.4.22 log.
- Multiplayer roadmap note (check for other nearby players before pulling a companion away from a shared fight, not just filtering by ownership) - acknowledged and captured for when multiplayer work starts; not implemented, `ReturnLogic`'s existing ownership filter (`CompanionInteract.IsOwned`) already covers the narrower "don't return to someone else's death" case.
- Build tag `v41-respawn-hook-fix`, version 1.4.23 (length-6 version string, same-length byte edit, decoded and confirmed: `id=com.profmags.companions`, `name=Offline Companions`, `version=1.4.23`, trailer `00 00`).

### Next steps
- Test dying near a companion again specifically. The log should now show `[Return] triggered` a second time, right around the actual death/respawn (not just once at session start) - that's the direct confirmation the `OnRespawn` hook fix worked.
- Shield logic: open design question for next round - should shield category be re-evaluated on some cadence even when *not* in combat (e.g. from `CompanionAI.UpdateAI`, similar to how `ReturnLogic.Tick` already runs every tick), so a companion swaps back to the round/parry shield soon after healing instead of waiting for the next fight? Or is the current "only re-check when a fight's weapon-selection tick runs" behavior fine and the real complaint is that the 30%-HP/3-enemy thresholds themselves feel too easy to trip? Need the user's read on which before writing anything.

## 1.4.24 — "return to me" now only pulls in companions actually set to Follow; sheathe-while-idle feature investigated and shelved
User clarified the auto-return-on-death feature should never affect a companion currently set to Farm/Forage/Stay/etc. - only ones set to Follow.
- **Fixed.** Added `ReturnLogic.IsInFollowMode(setupComp)`: reads the companion's live ZDO `ActionMode` int directly (same field `CompanionSetup.ApplyFollowMode`'s switch already keys off - `ActionModeHash`, via reflection since the hash's actual value is only known at runtime) and compares it to `0` (`ModeFollow`). `Trigger()` - the single method both the manual "come to me" key and the new `OnPlayerRespawn()` auto-pull funnel through - now checks this first and returns immediately (logging `"[Return] skipped for ... - not currently set to Follow, left alone"`) for any companion in another mode, before touching its follow state or engagement. Fails open (treats as Follow) only if reflection can't find the fields at all, so a lookup failure can't newly trap a companion - same "fail open" posture the rest of `ReturnLogic` already uses. This protects both the automatic on-respawn trigger and the manual key equally, since the user's concern (a working companion shouldn't abandon its job) applies to both.
- Compiled via `mcs`, spliced in as a full class-body replacement for `ReturnLogic` (safest option once a class needs several related changes, same pattern as previous full-class splices this session). `ilasm` reassembled clean; verified via `monodis` that the class list and new method are present, and via UTF-16 string dump that the build tag/version updated.
- **Sheathe-weapons-while-idle (for stamina) - investigated, shelved for now.** Checked directly against the game assembly before writing anything: (1) `CompanionStamina` only drains for running/sneaking/swimming, nothing drains for merely holding a weapon out; (2) the "running with a weapon out is slower" behavior the user was thinking of is real, but it's base-game logic on `Player.GetEquipmentMovementModifier()` specifically - the base `Character` class (what companions actually use) just returns a hard-coded `0` for that method, never overridden for companions, nothing this mod touches. So sheathing a companion's weapon would be purely cosmetic (`Humanoid.HideHandItems()`/`ShowHandItems()` - vanilla API, would have been the right tool) with zero effect on stamina or movement speed as things stand. User asked to skip it for now given that, undecided on whether to revisit as a cosmetic-only feature later.
- Build tag `v42-return-follow-mode-gate`, version 1.4.24 (length-6 version string, same-length byte edit, decoded and confirmed: `id=com.profmags.companions`, `name=Offline Companions`, `version=1.4.24`, trailer `00 00`).

### Next steps

Then test a Follow companion dying nearby to confirm that path still works as in 1.4.23.

## 1.4.25 — diagnosed the "companion just appeared" report as expected behavior (home base = bed location); added source-tagged diagnostics for the two respawn hooks
User sent a log from 1.4.24 with two player deaths and the same "it just appeared next to me instead of running" observation from before.
- **First death - traced and explained, not a bug.** The companion was set to mode 15 (Repair, StayHome) at the time, sitting in a chair at its home base for an 89s rest cycle (`[HomeLife|Companion] Sat down for 89s`). The player's own bed ("Found bed at custom spawn point") is at essentially the same coordinates as that chair - the companion's minimap pin re-appears within 0.1m of where it already was. It "just appeared" because home base and the respawn bed are the same location, not because anything teleported or ran. `[Return] skipped for "..." - not currently set to Follow, left alone` confirms the 1.4.24 fix worked correctly here.
- **Second death - a real gap found while checking the first.** This time the companion *was* set to Follow, but no `[Return]` line - triggered or skipped - appeared at all. Traced into the actual game assembly (not guessed): `Player.OnSpawned` (the original hook `PlayerHooks` patches) is called from a normal, unconditional code path on every respawn (`Game.SpawnPlayer`, called from `Game.UpdateRespawn`, confirmed via `monodis` on `assembly_valheim.dll`) - so it should be reliable. `Player.OnRespawn` (the hook added in 1.4.23/1.4.24 to fix the original no-trigger-on-death bug) has **zero normal callers anywhere in the game assembly** - it's likely invoked through an animation/UnityEvent callback rather than a plain method call, which would explain firing inconsistently rather than every time. Since both hooks funnel into the same `[Return]` log message, there was no way to tell from the log which one (if either) produced the first death's line, or why the second death produced nothing.
- **Fixed (diagnostics, not behavior):** `ReturnLogic.OnPlayerRespawn()` split into a thin wrapper (still called by `PlayerHooks.Postfix`/`OnSpawned`, tags itself `"OnSpawned"`) and a new `OnPlayerRespawnFrom(string source)` (called by `RespawnHooks.FullRestore()`/`OnRespawn`, tags itself `"OnRespawn"`), both funneling into one method. Added log lines - `"[Return] <source>: respawn hook fired"` unconditionally at the top, plus a labeled reason for every previously-silent early exit (`Player.m_localPlayer` null, `CompanionSetup` type not found, `get_AllCompanions` not found, `AllCompanions` returned null, `AllCompanions` was empty, or every companion found was unowned). Nothing about the actual pull-in behavior changed - this is purely additive instrumentation, same "log why, don't guess" pattern as `AIGateDiag`. Compiled via `mcs`, spliced in as a full-class-body replacement for both `ReturnLogic` and `RespawnHooks` (both changed, contiguous in the file, done as one splice). `ilasm` reassembled clean; verified via `monodis` all four related classes (`PlayerHooks`, `PlayerRespawnHooks`, `ReturnLogic`, `RespawnHooks`) are present and via UTF-16 string dump that the new log strings and version/build tag are baked in.

If `OnSpawned` itself sometimes doesn't log "fired" on a death-respawn, that would be a more surprising finding worth its own follow-up.

## 1.4.26 — shields excluded from AutoEquipBest, distance-capped return with a teleport-recall fallback, and party-follow while the owner is dead
Three fixes shipped together after a design discussion (no new log evidence needed for these - the root causes were fully diagnosed in 1.4.25's session; this round just builds the fixes). The `AIGateDiag`-based "companion just stands there and won't even fight" report is a separate, still-open thread — the user hasn't seen it in the last two tests and will keep watching for a log if it recurs.

**1. Shield conflict — fixed for real this time.** Root cause (found in 1.4.25's session): `CompanionSetup.AutoEquipBest()` runs on every inventory change and always queues the single highest-raw-`m_blockPower` shield it finds, completely bypassing `ShieldLogic`/`CompanionCombatAI.TryEquipShield`'s HP/threat-aware category logic - that's why the tower shield kept winning regardless of what `ShieldLogic` actually wanted equipped. Per the user's explicit choice ("Exclude shields from AutoEquipBest entirely"): `AutoEquipBest`'s internal item-type dispatch `switch` (keyed on `m_itemType - 3`) had its shield case (`m_itemType == 5`, was `IL_020e`) redirected to the same "skip this item" target every uninteresting item type already uses (`IL_028a`). This means the best-shield local variable pair is never populated, and the existing (already-present) null-guard right before the shield-queueing code (`if (bestShield == null) skip`) takes over from there - no other code needed changing. `ShieldLogic`/`CompanionCombatAI` is now the sole thing that ever calls `EquipItem` on a shield.

**2. "Return to me" on a distant death — real walk-back/teleport behavior, not just diagnostics.** Building on 1.4.25's finding that a companion's zone unloads on a far-away death (so `AllCompanions` is empty right when the respawn hook fires), plus the follow-up discussion about *why* it "just appears" instead of running (the old `Trigger()` called `SetFollowTarget` with a live reference to the player no matter how far away that was, which would walk the companion toward the edge of whatever zone bubble was keeping it loaded and risk it unloading itself mid-walk):
   - `ReturnLogic` now checks distance before committing to a live chase. Within a new `MaxChaseDistance` (60m, roughly one loaded zone-group), it behaves exactly as before - `SetFollowTarget` is set and it walks back normally.
   - Beyond that, it holds position instead (no follow-target set, so it can't wander into an unloaded gap) and starts an 87-second grace clock (the user's chosen window - "1 min 27s").
   - If the player hasn't come within chase range by the time the clock expires, `Tick()` calls a new `DoTeleportRecall`: it reuses `CompanionManager.FindSpawnPosition(playerPos, 30f)` (the same navmesh-safe placement already used for the companion's own death-respawn, just with a bigger 30m search radius instead of DoRespawn's 4m) to find a spot near the player, moves the companion there (`transform.position` + `ZDO.SetPosition`, guarded by `nview.IsOwner()`), sets its follow target to the player so it visibly walks the last stretch in, and calls a new `CompanionSetup.HidePinFor(4f)` so the minimap icon doesn't just pop back up next frame (`UpdateMinimapPin` now checks a new `_pinHiddenUntil` field before recreating the pin) - meant to read as "it walked up" rather than "it popped in."
   - Immediately after landing, it says one of a small pool of greeting lines ("I've been looking for you!", "There you are!", "Finally found you!") via the existing `CompanionTalk.Say(string, bool)` system (the same one used for tombstone-found/recovered lines elsewhere in the mod) - found via `_talk` field reflection off `CompanionAI`, same cross-assembly pattern used everywhere else in the helpers. **Note:** these lines are hardcoded English strings, not run through the mod's `ModLocalization.Loc(...)` system - left that way to keep this round's scope contained; flag if localized versions are wanted later.
   - The old fixed grace-then-immediately-active `_active`/`_prevFollow` bookkeeping is unchanged; `MaxChaseDistance`/`TeleportGraceSeconds`/`TeleportRadius`/`PinHideSeconds` are all named constants at the top of `ReturnLogic`, easy to retune without touching logic.

**3. Party-follow while the owner is dead — new behavior.** From the user's report of playing with real friends: when they die, their companion (fully loaded and ticking the whole time, since friends are right there keeping its zone active) just stood idle instead of acting like part of the group. Root cause is different from #2 entirely - no zone-unload involved, this is purely "no valid owner to walk toward, and nothing tells it to do anything else."
   - New Harmony postfix `PlayerDeathHooks` on `Player.OnDeath()` (hand-IL, same byte-blob-reuse pattern as `PlayerHooks`/`PlayerRespawnHooks`, just a 7-byte `"OnDeath"` string instead of the two existing 9-byte hook names) calls `ReturnLogic.OnPlayerDeath(Player __instance)`. Since Harmony patches the method for every `Player` instance on the client (local or remote), the postfix takes `__instance` and only sets a new `ReturnLogic.OwnerDead` flag when `__instance == Player.m_localPlayer` - a friend dying nearby does not trip this.
   - `OwnerDead` is cleared at the top of `OnPlayerRespawnFrom` (fires reliably via `OnSpawned`, confirmed in 1.4.25), so the window is exactly "from your death to your respawn."
   - `ReturnLogic.Tick()` (already called every AI tick for every companion) now also runs `TickPartyFollow` whenever `OwnerDead` is true, the companion is in Follow mode, and it isn't already mid-return: it scans `Player.GetAllPlayers()` (the vanilla list of all currently-loaded Player instances, so it can never accidentally pick another companion - those aren't `Player`-typed) for the nearest real player within 40m (`PartyFollowRadius`, excluding the dead owner) and sets that as its temporary follow target, per the user's answer ("real player, dead-only, never another companion" - "using another NPC could end up in an unloaded state again or they start playing the game themselves"). Combat is untouched by any of this - it was already independent of the follow target (confirmed by tracing `CompanionAI.UpdateAI`: `FindEnemy`/`Engage`/`UpdateCombat` run every tick regardless of what's being followed, gated only by the existing `Passive stance` check), so it keeps fighting normally the whole time it's tagging along.
   - The moment the owner respawns, `Trigger()` (called for every owned companion from the respawn hook) unconditionally clears the temp-follow flag and re-points at the real owner - no separate hand-off code needed.

Build tag `v44-shield-exclude+return-teleport+party-follow`, version 1.4.26 (same-length byte edit: `4.25`→`4.26`). `ilasm` reassembled clean, no errors or warnings. Verified via `monodis --typedef` that `PlayerDeathHooks`, `ReturnLogic`, and `RespawnHooks` are all present with expected method counts, and via UTF-16 string dump that the new build tag/version strings are baked in. (A full-assembly `monodis` text dump segfaults partway through on both the old 1.4.25 `Companions.dll` and this new build equally - a pre-existing tool limitation on a file this size, not something introduced by these changes; `--typedef` and targeted method-region dumps don't hit it.)

### Next steps
- Test all three: shield choice should now track `ShieldLogic`'s HP/threat logic with no tower-shield override; a distant death should either walk back once you're within ~60m or get recalled near you (with a greeting line and a brief pin-hide) after ~87s if you never get that close; dying with friends nearby should have the companion follow one of them and keep fighting instead of standing idle.
- The `AIGateDiag`-tagged "just sits there and won't fight" report is still open - the user hasn't seen it in the last two tests, so it may already be resolved as a side effect of some other fix; keep watching, and if it recurs, a fresh log with the `[AIGate]` lines will say exactly which early-exit gate is firing instead of guessing.
- `PartyFollowRadius` (40m), `MaxChaseDistance` (60m), `TeleportGraceSeconds` (87s), `TeleportRadius` (30m), and `PinHideSeconds` (4s) are all easy to retune constants at the top of `ReturnLogic`/inline in `CompanionSetup.HidePinFor` if any feel off in practice.
- Greeting lines are hardcoded, not localized - revisit if that matters.
- The manual "come to me" key remains unbound (discussed, not decided) - `Trigger()`'s follow-mode gate makes an accidental press harmless now, so it's safer to bind than when this was first held off.
- Roadmap items not yet started: Passive-stance flee-to-player-if-following/flee-to-home-if-working; night light while foraging/gathering after dark; the 27 dead config entries; the optional distance-based temporary melee fallback for Ranged stance; promoting `ReturnLogic`'s constants to BepInEx config entries.
- Previously-agreed, not recently re-verified: torch opener should pick the *best* torch among all usable ones, not just the first found.

## 1.4.27 — found and fixed the real "won't swap off the bow" bug (direct orders bypassed the combat AI), plus the flee/shield fixes approved last round

**The user caught a bad diagnosis.** Last round's log analysis (from `19ce4b3f-LogOutput.log`) concluded the "companion held the bow and blocked instead of swapping to melee" symptom was caused by the flee branch in `CompanionCombatAI.UpdateCombat` freezing all gear decisions below 30% HP. The user pushed back: "Are you looking at the current log file? Never did fleeing happen in it." Re-checked directly against the log this round:
- **Confirmed: no flee occurred.** There is no `[Flee]`-tagged log line anywhere in the 189,000-line file (the flee branch had no logging at all — a genuine "log why, don't guess" gap), and the companion's health never actually drops in the trailing ~46,000 lines; every `[Combat] GetMaxHealth` line there is just **max HP** drifting down as food buffs expire, not damage. The flee-branch theory, as applied to that log, was wrong.
- **The real encounter:** at line 185664, the user issued a `[Direct]` attack command against a greyling. ~2,000 log-lines later there's exactly one `[Durability] Armor reduction` line (took 4.7 dmg, mostly blocked by armor), then the game quits a few seconds after. **Zero `[Ranged]`/`[EQUIPDIAG]`/`[Shield]` lines appear anywhere in that whole encounter** — the custom combat AI's weapon/shield dispatch (`SelectBestAttack`) never ran at all.

**Root cause, found by tracing the IL:** `CompanionCombatAI.UpdateCombat` starts with `if (!_engaged) return false;` — nothing else in the method runs until `_engaged` is true. `_engaged` is only ever set by `CompanionCombatAI.Engage(Character)`, which also resets weapon-switch cooldowns, timers, etc. `CompanionInteract.DirectAttack` (the code behind the player-issued attack command) was **never calling `Engage`** — it only poked `CompanionAI.m_targetCreature` directly and called `SetAlerted(true)`. There is a bridge in `CompanionAI.UpdateAI` that's supposed to notice a manually-set `m_targetCreature` and route it into `_combatAI.Engage(...)` — but that bridge only runs when `_combatAI.IsEngaged` is currently *false*, and only after `BaseAI.FindEnemy()` (vanilla's own detection) comes back empty. In other words: a direct-order attack was relying on a fallback path that vanilla enemy-detection or a stale "already engaged" flag could silently swallow, so the custom AI's weapon/ammo/shield/flee logic — the whole point of this mod's combat layer — could simply never engage for a player-issued attack command. Whatever the companion did in that fight (holding the bow, "blocking") was pure vanilla base-AI behavior with no ammo-fallback, no shield logic, and no flee logic in play at all.

**Fix — direct orders now use the exact same entry point as natural detection.** Added `CompanionAI.DirectEngage(Character target)`: calls `SetAlerted(true)` then unconditionally calls `_combatAI.Engage(target)` — the identical method the natural-detection path calls, so a direct order now always (re-)engages the custom combat AI regardless of whatever it was doing a moment ago, instead of depending on a field being noticed on some future tick. `DirectAttack` now calls this instead of touching `m_targetCreature`/`SetAlerted` directly. `CompanionCombatAI.Engage` now logs `[Combat] Engage -> target="..." stance=...` every time it's called (low-frequency — once per combat start, not spammy) — the same log line will now fire identically for a direct order or a naturally-detected enemy, so the next log will directly prove they're both going through the same logic, per the user's ask.

**The three approved flee/shield fixes, now built:**
1. New BepInEx config entry `FleeHealthPct` (Combat section), default **15%**, range 5%–60% — separate from `HealthRetreatPct` (still 30%, still just the shield-category switch). The flee branch in `UpdateCombat` now reads this instead of a hardcoded `0.3f` literal.
2. While fleeing, the companion now calls `TryEquipShield()` (the same shield-selection logic `SelectBestAttack` uses) before running from its target — so it can pick a defensive shield to cover its retreat. (`ShieldLogic.SelectShield` already logs every pick, so this shows up as ordinary `[Shield]` lines with no extra logging needed.)
3. "Defend if cornered, then keep trying to flee": `CompanionAI.FleeFrom`'s return value (a thin wrapper over vanilla `BaseAI.Flee`, which returns `false` when it can't find anywhere to run) is now checked instead of discarded. If flee succeeds, behavior is unchanged (skip normal combat that tick). If it fails, a new `[Flee] could not create distance (blocked/cornered) -> defending itself this tick, will keep trying to flee` line logs once and the method falls through into the normal combat flow instead of returning — so a cornered companion fights back with whatever it just equipped, and re-checks its health/flee decision fresh next tick.
4. Added `[Flee] triggered -> hp={0:P0} ... target="..."` logging at the point flee actually fires, closing the "this branch never logs anything" gap that made last round's diagnosis unverifiable in the first place.

Build tag `v45-direct-engage-fix+flee-config+flee-shield+flee-cornered-defend`, version 1.4.27 (`4.26`→`4.27`). `ilasm` reassembled clean, no errors or warnings. Verified via `monodis --typedef` (all classes present, no crash) and a UTF-16 string dump confirming the new build tag/version strings and all four new log-message strings are baked into the binary. (Targeted `monodis --filter`/`--method` dumps of the specific edited methods both crash on this file — same pre-existing large-file tool limitation as prior rounds, not new; relying on `ilasm`'s zero-error reassembly + typedef + string checks, consistent with how 1.4.26 was verified.)

### Next steps
- Test a direct-order attack (whatever the attack-command bind is) against something the companion is out of ammo for, and check the log for the new `[Combat] Engage` line firing, followed by normal `[Ranged]`/`[EQUIPDIAG]` weapon-swap lines — that confirms direct orders are now routing through the same logic as a naturally-detected enemy.
- Test getting a companion down near 15% HP in a fight it can't immediately escape and check for `[Flee] triggered`, a `[Shield]` pick right after it, and (if genuinely cornered) the new `[Flee] could not create distance` line.
- `FleeHealthPct` defaults to 15% but is configurable in the BepInEx config file if that number needs tuning after a real test.
- The `AIGateDiag`-tagged "just sits there and won't fight" report is still open from a few rounds back — no update this round either way.
- Roadmap items not yet started (unchanged): Passive-stance flee-to-player-if-following/flee-to-home-if-working; night light while foraging/gathering after dark; the 27 dead config entries; promoting `ReturnLogic`'s constants to BepInEx config entries; a possible second dedicated "return to me" keybind.
- Previously-agreed, not recently re-verified: torch opener should pick the *best* torch among all usable ones, not just the first found.

## 1.4.30 — bed claiming + no-bed complaint

User reported companions would take over a player's own personal bed, and asked for them to "claim" a bed instead - discussed two designs:
- **Real ownership** (`Bed.SetOwner`/`RPC_SetOwner`): rejected. This writes into the bed's actual networked ZDO using a synthetic non-player ID, which is real save-file/multiplayer-replicated state the vanilla game wasn't designed to hold, with unverifiable side effects and no clean "unclaim."
- **Fake/cosmetic claim** (chosen): don't touch the bed's real ownership at all. Track claims ourselves and patch only the display text.

Implementation, all in `CompanionRest` (private class, holds the bed-sleep logic) unless noted:
- `_bedClaimNames` — new `static Dictionary<int32,string>` (bed Unity instance ID -> companion display name), parallel to the pre-existing `_claimedBeds` (`HashSet<int32>`, in-memory only, resets naturally on scene reload since instance IDs aren't persisted anyway — same lifetime as our new dict, so no extra persistence work needed).
  - **Claimed** in `FinalizeSleep()` right alongside the existing `_claimedBeds.Add(...)` call, using `this._setup`'s Component name (falls back to the literal string `"Companion"` if `_setup` is somehow null).
  - **Released** in the "stopped sleeping" method (the one logging `[Rest] Stopped sleeping...`) right alongside the existing `_claimedBeds.Remove(...)` call.
- `FindNearbyBed()` — added a `bed.GetOwner() != 0` check (skip the candidate) right after the existing null-check on the found `Bed` component, before the `_claimedBeds`-based inter-companion dedup logic that was already there. This is the actual fix for "don't use someone else's bed" — a real player-owned bed (checked via the game's real `Bed.GetOwner()`) is now off-limits to companions, independent of the cosmetic dictionary above.
- `_needsBed` — new `bool` field, set `true` in `TryAutoSleep()` when `FindNearbyBed()` comes back null (i.e., it was time to sleep but no usable bed was found), `false` whenever a bed is found. Exposed via new public `NeedsBed()` getter method. Note: this flag is only updated on ticks where `TryAutoSleep` actually reaches the bed-search step (night, not in combat, not Stay-Home, scan-timer elapsed) - it does NOT get cleared on the various earlier-return paths (enemy nearby, daytime, etc.), so a companion that failed to find a bed last night may still show as "needs a bed" during the day until its next successful sleep. Minor, acceptable given how `SayContextual`'s priority chain works (higher-priority states like combat/hunger take precedence anyway).
- New Harmony patch class `BedClaimHoverTextPatch` (`[HarmonyPatch(typeof(Bed), "GetHoverText")]`, postfix) - looks up the hovered bed's instance ID in `_bedClaimNames`; if found, appends `" (claimed by <name>)"` to the vanilla hover text via `string.Format`. Purely cosmetic - reads our own dictionary, never touches `Bed`'s real fields.
- `SpeechConfig.NoBed` - new `string[]` category (5 default lines), added the same way as the existing `Hungry`/`Repair`/etc. categories. `CompanionTalk.get_NoBedLines()` mirrors the existing `get_HungryLines()`-style getters.
- `CompanionTalk` - added a cached `_rest` field (`GetComponent<CompanionRest>()` in `Awake()`, same pattern as its existing `_food`/`_setup` fields) so it can reach `CompanionRest.NeedsBed()`. In `SayContextual()`'s existing priority chain (Combat > Overweight > Hungry > NeedsRepair > ... work-mode lines), inserted a NeedsBed check right after the NeedsRepair block, before the work-mode switch - so it's a personal-state complaint at the same tier as hunger/repair, not tied to any specific work mode.

Compiled clean via `ilasm`, verified via `monodis --typedef` (new `BedClaimHoverTextPatch` class present, no crash) and `strings -e l` (new build tag, version string, and both `"{0} (claimed by {1})"` and one of the new NoBed lines confirmed baked in). Untested in-game as of delivery - next test should check: (1) a companion picks an unclaimed bed and its hover text shows "claimed by <name>" while in use, and clears after they get up; (2) a companion does NOT take a bed the player has personally claimed (hold-interact on it themselves first); (3) with zero available beds nearby, the companion eventually says a NoBed line at night.

## 1.4.31 — configurable auto-respawn timer on top of the existing manual tombstone resurrect

**User's ask, prompted by a challenge to an earlier ("resurrection is manual-only") conclusion:** "There is no respawn mechanism at all in the mod? Can you double check? ... I know I rez the NPC from their tombstone. Thats not always practical." Investigation found there *is* a full automatic-respawn subsystem in `CompanionManager` (`_respawnQueue`/`QueueRespawn`/`ProcessRespawns`/`DoRespawn`) — but it's never called anywhere. It's genuinely dead code, entirely superseded (not broken) by the active manual system, `CompanionTombstoneResurrect` (hold-interact on the tombstone). User chose **Option 2**: keep the manual system as-is and add a configurable timer on top of it that reuses the same tested resurrect logic, rather than reviving the old dead subsystem.

**Design, worked out with the user turn by turn (each point explicitly confirmed before building):**
- Respawn location: the companion's own claimed bed if it has one; otherwise world spawn, or optionally near the owner instead (config toggle, off by default). Manual tombstone resurrect is unchanged — always at the tombstone.
- Inventory: kept 100% by default now (a behavior change from the old always-drop-everything). Two new toggles: drop everything into the tombstone instead, and (sub-option) drop everything except currently-equipped gear.
- Tombstone lifecycle: if nothing was dropped (or it's empty after recovery), it's destroyed automatically — reusing logic `DoResurrect()` already had. If something was dropped, the tombstone stays as an ordinary lootable container and the companion will walk over and loot it themselves once back in range — capped at a configurable distance (default 50) specifically so they don't wander far enough to risk an unload; this only gates the decision to *commit* to going after it, not the walk itself once committed.
- No multi-tombstone tracking: confirmed with the user this should track only the single most-recent tombstone, matching how vanilla Valheim itself only meaningfully tracks a player's latest death marker (a player CAN have multiple tombstones, but the mod deliberately doesn't try to reconcile that for companions).
- Map pin: confirmed the tombstone already gets a normal minimap death-pin (pre-existing `Minimap.AddPin(..., PinType.Death, ...)` in `CreateCompanionTombstone`) — this extends "for free" to the new auto-respawn path, no separate work needed.
- Mid-implementation correction from the user: when inventory is kept (nothing left in the tombstone), the tombstone should visibly break/disappear rather than sit there as an empty container. Turned out `DoResurrect()` already had exactly this destroy-if-empty-else-keep logic from its dead-code days — reused rather than rebuilt.

**Implementation, low-risk because it reuses existing, already-tested logic wherever possible:**
- 6 new `ModConfig` entries (section "Respawn"): `AutoRespawnEnabled` (bool, default true), `AutoRespawnDelaySeconds` (float, default 300, range 30–3600), `AutoRespawnNearOwner` (bool, default false), `DropInventoryOnDeath` (bool, default false), `DropEquippedGearOnDeath` (bool, default false), `TombstoneRecoveryRange` (float, default 50, range 5–100).
- `CompanionRest.FinalizeSleep()` now persists the claimed bed's position onto the companion's own ZDO the moment a bed claim is committed (new `HomeBedPosHash`/`HasHomeBedHash`), because `_bedTarget` itself is transient and gets cleared as soon as a sleep session ends — reading it later at time-of-auto-respawn would almost always find nothing. Same pattern the mod already uses for `HomePos`.
- `CompanionSetup.OnCompanionDeath()`/`CreateCompanionTombstone(...)` now read that bed-claim data off the dying companion's ZDO and thread it through to `CompanionTombstoneResurrect.WriteRespawnData(...)`, alongside a new keep/drop inventory split: "keep" items are pulled into a temp `Inventory`, serialized via the game's own `Inventory.Save`+`ZPackage.GetBase64()` (not hand-rolled), and only the "drop" remainder is left for the pre-existing grave-transfer logic to see — so the old drop-to-grave code needed no changes at all.
- `CompanionTombstoneResurrect.DoResurrect()` → `DoResurrect(bool isAutoRespawn)`: manual calls pass `false` (unchanged tombstone-position behavior); the new auto-respawn timer in `Update()` calls it with `true`, which branches to bed/near-owner/world-spawn positioning, restores the serialized "keep" inventory via `Inventory.Load`, and — reusing the pre-existing "items remained after pullback" check — either destroys the tombstone or (when kept) writes this tombstone's ID onto the newly-spawned companion's ZDO so the dormant `CompanionAI.UpdateTombstoneRecovery` scan-and-loot logic picks it up.
- `CompanionAI.UpdateTombstoneRecovery(float dt)` — added the distance gate discussed above: a tombstone candidate farther than `TombstoneRecoveryRange` is skipped rather than committed to.
- `CompanionManager.GetWorldSpawnPoint()`/`FindSpawnPosition(...)` changed from `private static` to `assembly static` so `CompanionTombstoneResurrect` could call them directly instead of duplicating them.

Build tag `v49-auto-respawn-and-keep-inventory`, version 1.4.31 (`4.30`→`4.31`). Every edit reassembled clean via `ilasm` on the first attempt; final build (`Companions_1431.dll`) also reassembled clean. Verified via `monodis --typedef` (clean, no crash) and `strings -e l` (confirmed all 6 new config keys, the build tag, the version string, and the `[Resurrect] Restored kept inventory` log line are baked in). A full `monodis --method` dump aborted partway through with the same pre-existing large-file crash (`dump.c:604` assertion, exit 134) seen in earlier rounds — not new, not tied to this edit; the clean `ilasm` reassembly (which validates every method, including the changed ones) plus the `--typedef`/`strings` checks are the standing verification method for this DLL. Untested in-game as of delivery.

### Hotfix (same version, build tag `v49b-auto-respawn-maxstack-fix`) — actual in-game crash found and fixed

First real in-game test surfaced two bugs from the log the user attached (`e45515dc-LogOutput.log`):

1. **The real bug, and the one causing the reported symptom** ("NPC did not become a tombstone and is stuck in place at 0 HP"): `InvalidProgramException: Invalid IL code in Companions.CompanionSetup:OnCompanionDeath (): IL_02d4: ldloc.s 26`, thrown from `Character.CheckDeath()` the instant a companion's `OnDeath` event fires — i.e. before `CreateCompanionTombstone` ever runs. Root cause: `OnCompanionDeath()`'s `CreateCompanionTombstone(...)` call site was extended this version with 2 new trailing args (`hasHomeBed`, `homeBedPos`), pushing `this` + 20 arguments onto the stack simultaneously (21 slots) right before the call — but the method's `.maxstack` directive was left at its old value, `19`. The verifier correctly rejected the method as invalid IL (not just "unverifiable" — genuinely malformed, since the declared max stack depth is smaller than what the code actually needs), so the whole death handler bailed out with an exception every single time, after already having set `_dead = true` — hence "no tombstone, but stuck in place at 0 HP forever." Fix: `.maxstack` raised `19` → `21`. (Every earlier `ilasm` reassembly passed because `ilasm` doesn't statically verify stack depth against `.maxstack` at assembly time the way the CLR verifier does at load time — this only ever surfaces at runtime, which is why it slipped through the project's usual `ilasm`-clean + `monodis --typedef` + `strings` verification and needed an actual in-game test to catch.)
2. **A separate, non-fatal bug**, spamming the log every time a player looked at a bed: `FieldAccessException: Field 'Companions.CompanionRest:_bedClaimNames' is inaccessible from method 'Companions.BedClaimHoverTextPatch:Postfix'` — a leftover from 1.4.30. `_bedClaimNames` was declared `private static` on `CompanionRest`, but `BedClaimHoverTextPatch` (a different class) reads it directly in its Harmony postfix to build the "(claimed by ...)" hover text. Fix: changed the field's accessibility from `private static initonly` to `assembly static initonly` (internal-equivalent), matching the pattern already used for cross-class hash fields elsewhere in the mod. Harmony/the hover-text postfix silently no-ops on this exception rather than crashing anything, so this bug was cosmetic (no "claimed by" text ever showed) rather than the cause of the reported symptom.

Both fixes verified via clean `ilasm` reassembly (zero errors) and `monodis --typedef`/`strings -e l` (new build tag `v49b-auto-respawn-maxstack-fix` confirmed present). Not yet re-tested in-game as of this delivery — next test is the same death/tombstone/respawn loop that originally surfaced the crash.

### Next steps
- Test the full loop: let a companion die with default settings (keep inventory, auto-respawn on), confirm it respawns at its claimed bed (or world spawn if it never claimed one) after ~5 minutes with all its items, and that the tombstone is gone.
- Test `DropInventoryOnDeath` on: confirm items land in the tombstone, the tombstone stays as a lootable container with a death-pin on the map, and the respawned companion walks over and loots it once within 50m — and does NOT commit to the walk from farther away.
- Test `DropEquippedGearOnDeath` sub-option: confirm only equipped gear survives into the new companion's inventory and everything else lands in the tombstone.
- Test manual tombstone hold-resurrect still works unchanged and cancels/no-ops the auto-respawn timer for that companion.
- Test `AutoRespawnNearOwner`: confirm it only applies when the companion has no claimed bed.
- Everything from 1.4.30's "Untested in-game" bed-claim checks is also still open.

## 1.4.32 — persistent, player-like bed claims (replaces 1.4.30's transient "occupied right now" model)

**User's ask, after 1.4.31's tombstone/respawn work shipped:** a companion should claim a bed like a player would — a standing assignment, not something that only exists while they're actually lying in it. Specifically: claim automatically if one's available and they don't have one; claim near wherever Home Position gets set (and re-claim if it moves); notice if their owner has personally settled into a bed nearby and get one of their own too, "like a real player would"; and pointing at a bed + commanding stays available as a manual override/fallback for assigning or updating a specific bed. Corrected mid-discussion: the mod already "fakes" bed ownership display rather than touching the game's real `Bed.SetOwner` (established back in 1.4.30, for good reason — real ownership is real networked save state we don't want to touch) — the ask here is for that fake claim to be **permanent-until-reassigned**, not toggle on and off with sleep the way 1.4.30 had it.

**Design confirmed with the user, piece by piece:**
- A claim is now a standing assignment (tracked on the companion's own ZDO, same spirit as Home Position), not "currently occupied." The hover text ("(claimed by \<name\>)") now reflects this permanently, not just while asleep.
- Automatic claim triggers: (1) `SetHomePosition` — immediate search within Home Zone Radius of the new point; (2) a recurring ~30s background check for a home-based companion with no valid claim; (3) noticing a real player's own claimed bed within ~20m when the companion has no claim of their own yet — confirmed this should grab a bed, not also silently set Home Position (that stays an explicit, separate action).
- Follow-only companions with no Home Position simply don't auto-claim via (1)/(2) — only via (3), the owner-bed-proximity trigger — until the player either Sets Home for them or manually assigns a bed via point-command.
- Claiming a new bed always releases whatever the companion had claimed before, freeing it for someone else.
- Point-command on a bed is the manual override: unclaimed → claim + sleep; already theirs → sleep; someone else's (real player, or a different companion) → refuse and speak a new "that's not my bed" line, don't walk over.
- Confirmed zone-loading has no bearing on any of this: Valheim's `ZoneSystem` loads/unloads per distance-from-any-player, not per base, and nothing (physics scans, `Update()` ticks) runs for a companion whose zone is unloaded — so the 30s check and owner-bed check simply don't fire while unloaded, and resume cold on next load with no drift to account for.

**Implementation** (`CompanionRest` unless noted):
- New field `_claimedBed` (the standing claim, `Bed` reference) alongside the existing `_claimedBeds`/`_bedClaimNames` static dictionaries (now genuinely persistent claims, not released each morning) and new `_bedClaimScanTimer` (float, 30s cadence).
- `FindNearbyBed` kept as the original 20m-around-current-position search (still used nowhere new); added `FindAnyBedNear(Vector3 anchor, float radius)` (nearest bed regardless of owner — used to relink a claim by stored position after a zone reload), `FindUnclaimedBedNear(Vector3, float)` (nearest bed that isn't a real player's and isn't already claimed by someone else — the one actual auto-claim searches use), and `HasOwnerBedNearby(Vector3, float)` (true if any real player has a claimed bed in range — used for the owner-proximity trigger; deliberately checks "a real player's bed", not specifically the companion's own registered owner's bed, since `Bed.GetOwner()` alone doesn't require resolving which specific player owns this companion — a reasonable simplification worth knowing about for a busy multiplayer server with several owners based near each other).
- `ClaimBed(Bed)` — releases the current claim (`ReleaseClaimedBed()`), sets `_claimedBed`, adds to `_claimedBeds`/`_bedClaimNames`, and writes `HomeBedPosHash`/`HasHomeBedHash` onto the companion's own ZDO (the same fields 1.4.31 added for auto-respawn — now written at claim time, not only after they finish a sleep cycle, so a claimed-but-not-yet-slept-in bed is still a valid respawn point).
- `ReleaseClaimedBed()` — removes the current claim from both dictionaries and clears `_claimedBed`.
- `TryClaimBedIfNeeded()` (public, called cross-class from `CompanionSetup.SetHomePosition` and from `CompanionRest.Update`'s new 30s timer) — if the current claim was taken over by a real player, releases it; otherwise, if `HasHomeBedHash` is set but nothing untoward happened, tries to relink to the actual `Bed` object at the stored position (handles the case where the claim survived a zone reload but the in-memory `_claimedBed` reference didn't); if there's still no live claim, searches near Home Position (if set) or, if not, only proceeds when `HasOwnerBedNearby` says the owner has settled in nearby — then claims the nearest unclaimed bed found.
- `CompanionSetup.SetHomePosition` — after writing the ZDO fields (unchanged), now also calls `GetComponent<CompanionRest>().TryClaimBedIfNeeded()`.
- `CompanionRest.Update` — added the 30s timer tick calling `TryClaimBedIfNeeded()`, alongside the existing per-second dispatch.
- `TryAutoSleep()` — no longer calls `FindNearbyBed()` for "any unclaimed bed nearby." Now: if there's no live claim, tries `TryClaimBedIfNeeded()` once; if still no claim, sets `_needsBed = true` and returns (the 1.4.30 "no bed" complaint fires, as intended — they will not wander to whatever's nearby); if there is a claim, sleeps in it via the existing `BeginSleep(bed, isAuto: true)`.
- `CompanionRest.DirectSleep(Bed)` changed from `void` to `bool` (accepted/refused): null bed → true (no-op, not a refusal); a real player's bed (`GetOwner() != 0`) → false; already this companion's `_claimedBed` → proceed; claimed by someone else (in `_claimedBeds` but not this companion's) → false; otherwise unclaimed → `ClaimBed(bed)` then proceed. `CompanionInteract.DirectSleep` (the point-command dispatcher) now checks this return value and, on `false`, has the companion speak a `get_NotMyBedLines()` line instead of walking over.
- New localized speech category `hc_cmd_notmybed_1/2/3` ("That's not my bed." / "That one's already taken." / "That's not mine to use."), registered the same way as the existing `hc_cmd_wake_*`/`hc_cmd_sleep_*` lines, with a matching `CompanionInteract.get_NotMyBedLines()` getter.
- `StopSleeping()` — the old "release the claim every time they get up" code (`_claimedBeds.Remove`/`_bedClaimNames.Remove`, alongside the `[Rest] Stopped sleeping...` log line) was removed outright, with a comment explaining why: claims are persistent now and only release when a different bed gets claimed or a real player takes the bed over, not just because morning came.
- `FinalizeSleep()`'s original claim-on-arrival code (from 1.4.30) was left in place rather than removed — it's now redundant with `ClaimBed` (which already ran before the companion ever started walking there) but harmless, since re-adding to a `HashSet`/re-setting the same `Dictionary` key is a no-op.

**Verification, upgraded after 1.4.31's maxstack bug slipped through the usual checks:** this round, every new/modified method's `.maxstack` was manually traced by hand (the same technique that caught the 1.4.31 bug — counting the live stack depth at the worst point, usually a call with many arguments) rather than trusting a clean `ilasm` reassembly alone, since `ilasm` does not itself verify declared `.maxstack` against actual required depth. On top of that, built a small standalone verification harness (`verify_all.cs`, using `System.Runtime.CompilerServices.RuntimeHelpers.PrepareMethod` via reflection against all the game's real reference assemblies) that force-JITs every method in the assembly and reports any failure — confirmed via a deliberate reintroduction of the exact 1.4.31 maxstack bug that this harness does *not* reliably catch that specific class of bug under Mono's normal (non-dynamic-method) JIT path, which doesn't always enforce full stack verification for a directly-loaded assembly the way it does for Harmony's dynamic-method-based patches — so it's a useful smoke test (ran clean, 1553/1553 methods) but not a substitute for the manual maxstack trace, which remains the real check for this specific bug class. `monodis --typedef` and `strings -e l` both confirm as usual (new build tag, version, and all new strings present). Untested in-game as of delivery.

### Next steps
- Test the four claim triggers: Set Home near an available bed; the ~30s idle-with-no-claim check; noticing the owner's own claimed bed nearby (Follow companion, no Home Position); and point-command claiming a specific bed.
- Test the refusal path: point-command a companion onto a bed the player is personally using, and onto a bed a different companion already claimed — confirm the "not my bed" line fires and they don't walk over.
- Test reassignment: change Home Position to a new location with a different available bed nearby, confirm the old bed's hover text clears (no longer "claimed by") and the new one picks it up.
- Test that a companion with no claim and no nearby owner bed correctly just says the existing "no bed" line at night instead of grabbing whatever's closest.
- Confirm the claimed bed still serves as the 1.4.31 auto-respawn point even when the companion claimed it but never actually got a chance to sleep in it yet.

## 1.4.29 — game-patch API compatibility fix

Hexium's compatibility checker flagged the published 1.4.28 listing as broken against the current game build (Steam app 896660, build 25527701, refs 77e4db6efc378469) with 11 findings, all "method no longer exists":
- `Inventory.AddItem(string, int32, int32, int32, int64, string, bool)->ItemDrop+ItemData` — 3 call sites (CookController and HomesteadController crafting-into-inventory paths).
- `Piece.SetCreator(int64)` — 1 call site (FarmController placing a plant/piece).
- `VisEquipment.SetRightItem/SetLeftItem/SetChestItem/SetLegItem/SetHelmetItem/SetShoulderItem/SetUtilityItem` — all 7, in `CompanionPanel::ApplyItemToVis`.

The user supplied a fresh `Managed.zip` from their game install (dated Sep 25, the day of the patch) to confirm the actual current signatures rather than guessing from the checker's text alone:
- `AddItem` gained a trailing `[opt] bool pickedUp` param (now 8 params total) — fixed by pushing `ldc.i4.0` (false) before the call at all 3 sites, matching prior behavior (these are all "craft a fresh item into inventory" cases, not world pickups).
- `SetCreator` gained a second `valuetype [Splatform]Splatform.PlatformUserID` param — fixed by adding a new local of that type, `initobj`-ing it to a default/empty value, and passing it. Companions aren't real platform accounts, and this only affects piece-attribution metadata, not gameplay.
- The `VisEquipment.Set*Item` methods switched from `string` to `int32 itemHash` — fixed by inserting a call to the already-used `class [assembly_utils]StringExtensionMethods::GetStableHashCode(string)` helper (same pattern the mod already used for `SetHairItem`/`SetBeardItem`) before each call, and updating each callsite's target signature. `SetLeftItem` and `SetShoulderItem` also gained a `variant` param in the new signature (making them 3-arg instead of 2-arg) — passed `0` for it, matching the `quality` value those calls already passed before.

Verified via clean `ilasm` reassembly, `monodis --typedef` (no crash), and `strings -e l` confirming the new build tag/version strings are baked in. Untested in-game as of delivery — next test should re-check normal companion crafting (cook/homestead), plant/piece placement, and gear swap visuals (the radial-wheel inventory panel) still work correctly.

## 1.4.28 — fixed the actual "stays at bow range while wielding melee" bug (a movement/kiting issue, separate from the weapon-equip fix in 1.4.27)

**1.4.27's fix worked as intended.** The new `446ea847-LogOutput.log` confirms it directly: `[Combat] Engage -> target="$enemy_greydwarf" ... ` now fires for `[Direct]` attack-commanded targets, same as naturally-detected ones. The ammo-fallback logic itself is also working correctly — `[Ranged] FindReadyBow -> none ... falling back to melee` fires, `TryForceMeleeWeapon` equips the axe, and `TryEquipShield` equips a shield right after, all logged normally. So the *weapon* really is switching to the axe during the fallback. Good.

**But the user reported a different, still-real symptom:** "When the NPC falls back to melee they still try and stay at bow range." Traced this to `CompanionCombatAI.UpdateCombat`'s attack-range/positioning calculation (the `isRanged` flag that later drives how much distance the AI tries to keep from its target). The logic was:
```
if weapon == null:                    isRanged = (_stance == 2)   // stance fallback
elif weapon.aiAttackRange > 10:       isRanged = true
elif weapon.itemType == Bow:          isRanged = true
else (weapon present, clearly melee): isRanged = (_stance == 2)   // BUG: same stance fallback
```
The last branch was the bug: whenever the currently-equipped weapon was present but neither long-range nor a bow (i.e. unambiguously melee, like the axe during an ammo-fallback), the code still fell back to checking the companion's **configured** combat stance (`_stance`) instead of just concluding "this is melee." Since the companion's configured stance stays `2` (Ranged) the whole time — swapping to the axe is a *temporary tactical fallback*, not a stance change — `isRanged` came out `true` even while holding the axe. Downstream, `isRanged` drives the attack range used for positioning (forces a `30` fallback range instead of the weapon's real short melee range), which is exactly "stand off at bow range" behavior.

**Fix:** the stance-based fallback now only applies when there's genuinely no weapon equipped (a real transient state, e.g. mid-swap). Any time a weapon **is** equipped and it isn't long-range or a bow, `isRanged` is now unconditionally `false` — the actual equipped weapon decides ranged-vs-melee positioning, never the configured stance. This is a small, surgical branch fix in `UpdateCombat`, no new config or logging added (this runs every combat tick, so a log line here would spam — the fix is provable in-game by watching the companion actually close distance, rather than in the log).

Build tag `v46-melee-fallback-range-fix`, version 1.4.28 (`4.27`→`4.28`). `ilasm` reassembled clean, no errors or warnings; `monodis --typedef` shows all classes present with no crash; version/build strings confirmed via UTF-16 string dump.

### Next steps
- Test: run the companion out of arrows against something, and confirm it now closes to melee range with the axe instead of hovering at bow distance. `[Ranged]`/`[EQUIPDIAG]` logs already confirm the weapon swap is correct — this round is purely about whether the companion's *movement* now matches.
- Everything else from 1.4.27's next-steps list is still open/unchanged (flee-threshold test, `AIGateDiag` "just sits there" report, roadmap items).
